# RPSC First Grade English Literature: Part 1

## Main study material

Introduction

Welcome to “RPSC First Grade English Literature: A Comprehensive Guide (Part 1)” – a meticulously crafted resource designed to illuminate the path for aspiring educators and literature enthusiasts alike. As an experienced educator and passionate advocate for English literature, I am thrilled to present this first installment of a comprehensive compilation of thousands of Multiple Choice Questions (MCQs) that span the vast and fascinating landscape of English literature.

Purpose and Scope

This book, Part 1 of the series, serves as an indispensable companion for those preparing for the Rajasthan Public Service Commission (RPSC) First Grade Teacher examination in English Literature. It is specifically written to align with the RPSC First Grade syllabus, ensuring that every question and explanation is relevant to your exam preparation. The questions, prepared by Mukesh K Sharma, are carefully crafted to mirror the style and depth of those asked in the actual RPSC examination, providing you with authentic practice and insights into the exam pattern.

Topics Covered in Part 1

This volume focuses on the following key areas of English literature:

Major Literary Works and Authors

The Vendor of Sweets by R.K. Narayan

On His Blindness by John Milton

Go and Catch a Falling Star by John Donne

Daffodils by William Wordsworth

Ode to the West Wind by Percy Bysshe Shelley

Ode on a Grecian Urn by John Keats

My Last Duchess by Robert Browning

Night of the Scorpion by Nissim Ezekiel

The Dance of the Eunuchs by Kamala Das

In Praise of Chimney Sweepers by Charles Lamb

Cry, the Peacock by Anita Desai

Literary Forms and Genres

Ode, Elegy, Ballad, Sonnet, Epic, Gothic, Allegory

Figures of Speech and Literary Devices

Detailed Study of Select Plays

Macbeth by William Shakespeare

Part 2 will further expand on the remaining topics, ensuring a holistic preparation experience. Together, both parts aim to cover the entire syllabus comprehensively.

For the Aspiring Educator

This guide is not just a collection of questions but a reflection of the depth required to truly appreciate and teach literature. It aims to equip you with the knowledge and analytical skills essential for inspiring future generations of literature lovers. The explanations provided with each MCQ serve as mini-lessons, demonstrating how to approach literary analysis and convey complex ideas concisely.

Happy reading, and best of luck in your endeavours!

Mukesh K Sharma
M.A., B.Ed., M.Phil., NET, SET
Lecturer in English, Government of Rajasthan
Fulbright International Award for International Teacher (USA) Winner
Model Educator Award Winner, Government of Rajasthan
Key Faculty in E-Kaksha, an initiative of Government of Rajasthan

Vendor of Sweets

Author: R.K. Narayan
Publication Year: 1967

"The Vendor of Sweets" is a funny and touching story by famous Indian writer R.K. Narayan. It takes place in a made-up town called Malgudi, which appears in many of Narayan's books. The story is about old and new ways of life clashing in a changing India.

The main character is Jagan, a 55-year-old man who sells sweets in Malgudi. His wife has died, and he runs a successful sweet shop. Jagan is a mix of old and new ideas. He follows Gandhi's simple way of life, but he's also proud of being rich from his business.

Jagan's life changes when his son, Mali, comes back from America. Mali brings his girlfriend, Grace, who is half-Korean and half-American. Mali wants to start a business making machines that write books. He asks his dad for money to help. This causes problems between Jagan's old-fashioned values and Mali's modern, Western ideas.

Main Characters:

Jagan: The main character, a sweet seller in his mid-fifties. He's torn between Gandhi's ideas and being a businessman and father.

Mali: Jagan's son, who comes back from America with big plans and a foreign girlfriend. He likes Western ways and wants to make money.

Grace: Mali's girlfriend, who finds it hard to fit in with Indian culture and Jagan's traditional home.

Cousin: Jagan's relative who listens to his problems and gives advice.

Ambika: Jagan's dead wife, who still affects how he thinks and acts.

The book uses lots of symbols. The sweet shop stands for old Indian culture, with Jagan's sweets being like old values and customs. Mali's book-writing machine stands for new Western technology that might make people less creative.

Narayan writes in a simple, funny way. He uses irony to show how silly both old and new ways of thinking can be. For example, Jagan tries to live simply like Gandhi but also runs a big business, which is kind of funny.

The story goes in order, but sometimes it jumps back to show Jagan's memories, especially about his wife, Ambika. These memories help us understand why Jagan acts the way he does.

One big theme in the book is how Jagan and Mali don't understand each other. This isn't just about them; it shows how all of India was changing after becoming independent. Jagan likes old ways, but Mali likes new Western ideas. This causes problems between them.

Another important theme is finding yourself. Jagan learns about himself throughout the story. He starts to see that some of his ideas don't make sense together. In the end, he decides to leave his business and money to live a simpler life.

The book also talks about keeping your culture when the world is changing. Grace has trouble fitting in with Indian culture. Through her, we see Indian customs from an outsider's view.

Narayan uses Indian English and local sayings in his writing. This makes the story feel real but still easy for people from other countries to read. It fits with the book's idea of balancing old and new ways.

The town of Malgudi is important in the story. It's like a small version of India, where old and new ideas exist together, sometimes causing problems. This shows how India was changing after becoming independent.

As the story goes on, Jagan changes a lot. At first, he's very set in his ways. But he starts to question what he believes and does. This shows that people can change, even when they're older.

The ending of the book is open, making readers think about what might happen next to the characters and to India. Jagan's decision to leave everything and go on a spiritual journey could be seen as running away or as finding himself.

Narayan uses different writing techniques in the book:

Metaphors: The sweet shop is like old culture, the book-writing machine is like new Western ideas.

Irony: Used to show funny things about Jagan and some social customs.

Symbols: Things like Jagan's spinning wheel stand for old values, while Mali's foreign clothes stand for new Western culture.

Personification: Narayan sometimes writes about Malgudi as if it were a person.

"The Vendor of Sweets" is written in normal language, not poetry, so it doesn't have rhymes or special structure. But Narayan's writing has a nice rhythm and sounds like how Indian people speak English.

In the end, "The Vendor of Sweets" is a great book about old and new ideas clashing in India after it became independent. Through Jagan's family and the town of Malgudi, R.K. Narayan shows a changing society in a funny and thoughtful way. People still like the book because it talks about things that happen everywhere: parents and children not getting along, finding out who you are, and trying to understand life when everything is changing.

## Main study material

Chapter 1

How much cash does Jagan earn monthly from spinning?

A) Less than five rupees

B) Around ten rupees

C) More than twenty rupees

D) He doesn't sell the excess yarn

Answer: A

What time does Jagan's counting hour approach?

A) Four o'clock

B) Five o'clock

C) Six o'clock

D) Seven o'clock

Answer: C

How old is Jagan's wooden chair?

A) Nearly a decade old

B) Nearly half a century old

C) Nearly a century old

D) Nearly two centuries old

Answer: C

How many drops of honey does Jagan find adequate?

A) Five

B) Ten

C) Twenty

D) Thirty

Answer: C

When are the shutters of the shop drawn?

A) Five o'clock

B) Six o'clock

C) Seven o'clock

D) Eight o'clock

Answer: C

How much Mysore Pak is left over?

A) One seer

B) Two seers

C) Half a seer

D) Five seers

Answer: B

How much jilebi is left over?

A) One seer

B) Two seers

C) Half a seer

D) Quarter seer

Answer: C

How long does it take Jagan to count the day's cash?

A) Five minutes

B) Ten minutes

C) Fifteen minutes

D) Twenty minutes

Answer: C

What does Jagan place on the picture frame of the goddess Lakshmi?

A) A garland of roses

B) A string of jasmine

C) A marigold wreath

D) A lotus flower

Answer: B

Where does Jagan's cousin go first when he visits?

A) The prayer room

B) Jagan's office

C) The kitchen

D) The veranda

Answer: C

What material are Jagan's clothes made from?

A) Cotton

B) Silk

C) Wool

D) Material he spins himself

Answer: D

What is Jagan's footwear made from?

A) Leather from an animal that died naturally

B) Synthetic materials

C) Cloth spun by himself

D) Rubber soles

Answer: A

Who helps Jagan tan the leather for his footwear?

A) His wife

B) His son

C) An old cobbler

D) He does it himself

Answer: C

What has Jagan given up since morning?

A) Wheat

B) Honey

C) Rice

D) Salt

Answer: D

According to Jagan, what should one eat instead of regular salt?

A) Sea salt

B) Rock salt

C) Natural salt

D) No salt at all

Answer: C

What does Jagan cook and take with honey and greens?

A) Rice

B) Stone-ground wheat

C) Oats

D) Barley

Answer: B

Who was the cousin talking to about Jagan's self-dependence?

A) The Tax Collector

B) The Cooperative Registrar

C) The Police Commissioner

D) The Bank Manager

Answer: B

When is the beggar allowed to be seen at the shop?

A) Mondays

B) Wednesdays

C) Fridays

D) Sundays

Answer: C

Where does the front-stall boy drop the cash he receives?

A) In a cashbox

B) In a long-necked bronze jug

C) Directly in Jagan's hands

D) In a safe under the counter

Answer: B

What question does Jagan routinely ask when his staff assembles at his desk?

A) "How much did we earn today?"

B) "Is everything cleaned and put away?"

C) "How much is left over?"

D) "Did any customer complain today?"

Answer: C

What book does Jagan read while sitting on his chair?

A) The Ramayana

B) The Upanishads

C) The Bhagavad Gita

D) The Vedas

Answer: C

Who is "Captain"?

A) The head cook

B) The front-stall boy

C) The watchman at the door

D) Jagan's son

Answer: C

What does Jagan say about the leftover Mysore Pak?

A) "Throw it away."

B) "Distribute it among the staff."

C) "That we can sell tomorrow."

D) "Use it in a new recipe."

Answer: C

What does Jagan say about the leftover jilebi?

A) "It will still be good tomorrow."

B) "Won't be so good tomorrow."

C) "Fry it again for tomorrow."

D) "Give it to the watchman."

Answer: B

What does Jagan love to see at the end of the day?

A) Happy customers

B) Clean, shining trays returning to the kitchen

C) A large profit in his ledger

D) His staff working hard

Answer: B

According to Jagan, what three things do sweets consist of?

A) Flour, sugar, and flavors

B) Milk, sugar, and nuts

C) Butter, flour, and syrup

D) Coconut, jaggery, and ghee

Answer: A

What is Jagan's table drawer padded with?

A) A folded towel

B) A piece of foam

C) Old newspapers

D) A velvet cloth

Answer: A

Where does Jagan record the cash that comes in after six o'clock?

A) In a ledger open for inspection

B) In a small notebook

C) On a slip of paper

D) He doesn't record it separately

Answer: B

What does Jagan do with the "free cash"?

A) Deposits it in the bank

B) Uses it for daily expenses

C) Gives it to his staff as a bonus

D) Converts it to crisp currency and stores it in the loft

Answer: D

Who checks the lock on the door at the end of the day?

A) Jagan himself

B) The head cook

C) The front-stall boy

D) The watchman, referred to as "Captain"

Answer: D

What did Jagan do with the last bit of leather after his wife's death?

C) Donated it to a mission

D) Kept it as a memento

Answer: C

D) After retiring from work

Answer: C

A) Reducing the quantity of sweets made

B) Giving discounts at the end of the day

C) Trying a new sweet the next day

D) Selling the leftovers to a nearby restaurant

Answer: C

Why was the wooden chair specially made by Jagan's father?

A) For family gatherings

B) For an Englishman who visited for astrology lessons

C) As a wedding gift for Jagan

D) As a display piece for the shop

Answer: B

What happened to Mr. Noble's signed portrait?

A) It was framed and hung in the shop

B) It was lost during a house move

C) The children played with it and it ended up in the loft

D) Jagan kept it in his desk drawer

Answer: C

A) He ignores it and continues reading

B) He sends the head cook to check

C) He cries out, "What is happening?"

D) He goes to the kitchen himself

Answer: C

How does Jagan view the cash that comes in after six o'clock?

A) As a bonus

B) As a tip for good service

C) As free cash, a self-generated category

D) As a contribution to charity

Answer: C

A) Wearing suits

B) Eating with a fork and knife

C) Drinking tea

D) Viewing oneself daily in a mirror

Answer: D

What does Jagan say one must do to conquer the self?

A) Meditate daily

B) Conquer taste

C) Reject material possessions

D) Follow a guru

Answer: B

What does the cousin say about the sugar situation?

A) The price may increase

B) There is a shortage

C) The quality has improved

D) The government is lowering the price

Answer: A

## Main study material

Chapter 2

What time did Jagan return home?

A) Around 7:00 PM

B) A little past 7:30 PM

C) Around 8:00 PM

D) Late in the night

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan walked back home shortly after 7:30 PM, during a lull in Market Road's activity.

What was the street dog doing on the roadside?

A) Barking loudly

B) Wagging its tail

C) Begging for food

D) Snoring on a heap of stone

Answer: D

Explanation: A street dog was found sleeping peacefully, snoring while resting on a pile of stones by the road.

What was the vagrant waiting for at the parapet?

A) Scraps of food from homes

B) People to give him money

C) Someone to offer him a job

D) The bus to arrive

Answer: A

Explanation: The vagrant would wait for dining leaves to be discarded from homes in Kabir Lane, which he would collect and clean to eat any remaining food.

Where did Jagan spot his son Mali?

A) On the southern steps of the Lawley statue

B) At the Truth Printing press

C) Walking along Market Road

D) Inside their home

Answer: A

Explanation: Mali was identifiable by his yellow shirt while sitting on the southern steps of the Lawley statue.

What was the book that Jagan was waiting to be published?

A) A novel about Malgudi

B) A biography of Sir Frederick Lawley

C) A book on Nature Cure and Natural Diet

D) A collection of his political essays

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan's magnum opus on Nature Cure and Natural Diet had been at the press for many years.

What were the students on the southern steps of the Lawley statue discussing?

A) Politics and current affairs

B) Philosophy and literature

C) Film stars, cricket, and fashion

D) Their academic studies

Answer: C

Explanation: The students were engaged in animated discussions about film stars, cricket, and current fashion trends in dress and deportment.

What did Jagan do after entering his house?

A) Went straight to his bedroom

B) Switched on the light in the front room

C) Greeted his wife and son

D) Prepared himself a cup of tea

Answer: B

Explanation: Upon entering, Jagan's first action was to turn on the front room light before removing his upper clothing.

What did Jagan do with his jibba after removing it?

A) Hung it on the wall

B) Placed it in a basket for washing

C) Folded it neatly and set it aside

D) Gave it to his wife to mend

Answer: B

Explanation: After removing his jibba, Jagan put it in a basket designated for the next day's washing.

What was Jagan's knowledge of astronomy limited to?

A) Identifying the major constellations

B) Determining the position of the Pole Star

C) Distinguishing between planets and stars

D) Understanding the movement of celestial bodies

Answer: B

Explanation: His astronomical knowledge only extended to locating the Pole Star using Orion's Belt or Sword as reference points.

What was the condition of the bathroom in Jagan's house?

A) Well-maintained and modern

B) Dilapidated and in need of repair

C) Adequate but lacking in privacy

D) Spacious and comfortable

Answer: C

Explanation: The bathroom had a gap in its door due to a warped wooden frame, compromising privacy despite being functional.

What was Jagan's father's theory about growing coconut trees?

A) They required deep watering

B) They needed constant sunlight

C) They thrived with the addition of salt

D) They were best grown in well-drained soil

Answer: C

Explanation: His father believed adding substantial amounts of salt to the planting pits was essential for coconut tree growth.

What was Jagan's view on using a toothbrush?

A) He preferred using a margosa twig

B) He believed toothbrushes were unhygienic

C) He thought toothbrushes were too expensive

D) He felt they could damage the tooth enamel

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan opposed toothbrushes, choosing instead to use twigs from the margosa tree for dental hygiene.

Where did Mali have his own room in the house?

A) In the attic

B) In the front wing

C) In a hall without ventilation

D) Adjacent to his parents' room

Answer: C

Explanation: Mali's room was a long hall without windows or ventilators, known as the "cool room."

What did Mali have in his room?

A) A bed and a desk

B) Books and building blocks

C) A television and some toys

D) A personal computer and electronic gadgets

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali's room contained a collection of books on a round table along with house-building blocks.

What did Jagan call the "extraordinarily lively firework" in the sky?

A) The North Star

B) Venus or Jupiter

C) The Milky Way

D) Sirius, the Dog Star

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan would alternately identify this bright celestial object as Venus or Jupiter, admiring it despite his uncertainty.

What was the practice in Jagan's house regarding the bathroom?

A) Everyone was expected to use it regularly

B) The family members avoided looking inside

C) It was only used for bathing, not other purposes

D) Guests were not allowed to use the bathroom

Answer: B

Explanation: Family members followed a generational practice of not looking through the gap in the bathroom door.

How did Jagan's father expand the house?

A) By building additions to the front

B) By constructing a separate wing

C) By demolishing the old hut and building from scratch

D) By gradually expanding from the back yard to the front

Answer: D

Explanation: The house grew progressively under his father's direction, extending from the backyard area toward the front.

What was the purpose of the parapet over the culvert?

A) For people to sit on

B) To prevent accidents

C) To collect rainwater

D) No specific purpose mentioned

Answer: A

Explanation: The parapet at the junction of Market Road and Lawley Extension served as a sitting place.

What was the doctor doing at the Krishna Dispensary?

A) Examining a patient

B) Cleaning the dispensary

C) Discussing with a colleague

D) Closing up the dispensary

Answer: A

Explanation: Dr. Krishna was observed at his table examining a patient's throat.

What was the purpose of the broad steps around the Lawley statue?

A) For people to climb up and view the statue

B) To provide seating for the neighborhood residents

C) To allow traffic to pass through the intersection

D) As a platform for political rallies

Answer: B

Explanation: The pedestal's broad steps functioned as park benches for both young and old neighborhood residents.

What was the purpose of the "Jutkas" mentioned passing by Jagan?

A) They were part of the city's public transportation

B) They were emergency vehicles responding to a call

C) They were private modes of transportation

D) They were commercial delivery vehicles

Answer: C

Explanation: Jutkas were among the various private vehicles, including cycles and cars, used for personal transportation.

What did Jagan do after passing through the courtyards and corridors?

A) Went to his bedroom

B) Stood gazing at the stars

C) Visited his son Mali's room

D) Checked on his wife's condition

Answer: B

Explanation: After moving through the house, Jagan paused at the back door to admire the starlit sky.

What was Mali's room called in those days?

A) The study room

B) The quiet room

C) The cool room

D) The private chamber

Answer: C

Explanation: Mali's windowless hall was historically referred to as the "cool room."

How did Mali's room allow him to observe the house?

A) Through a window overlooking the courtyard

B) From a strategic position near the kitchen

C) Without losing sight of all the goings on

D) Through a series of connecting doors

Answer: C

Explanation: The room's location enabled Mali to maintain privacy while observing household activities and conversations.

What was the effect of the tall coconut tree near the bathroom?

A) It provided shade and cooling

B) It damaged the roof with falling fronds

C) It created privacy from neighbors

D) It blocked the water supply

Answer: B

Explanation: The coconut tree's falling fronds and debris would impact the corrugated roof with loud thuds.

What did Jagan use for brushing his teeth?

A) A modern toothbrush

B) A margosa twig

C) Herbal powder

D) Salt water

Answer: B

Explanation: Each morning, Jagan would break off a twig from the margosa tree and chew its tip for teeth cleaning.

When did Jagan perform his morning routine?

A) At sunrise

B) At five in the morning

C) Before dawn

D) After breakfast

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan consistently began his daily routine at five o'clock in the morning.

What did Jagan call the margosa tree?

A) Sacred plant

B) Medicinal herb

C) Amrita or Sanjeevini

D) Natural healer

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan referred to the margosa as both Amrita (divine nectar) and Sanjeevini (life-restoring herb).

What did Mali's room contain for entertainment?

A) A collection of toys

B) Electronic gadgets

C) House-building blocks

D) Board games

Answer: C

Explanation: Among Mali's possessions were house-building blocks for recreational activities.

How did Jagan's wife respond to his natural remedies?

A) She embraced them enthusiastically

B) She followed them reluctantly

C) She refused to associate with them

D) She modified them to her liking

Answer: C

Explanation: His wife completely rejected Jagan's health-related theories and maintained her own independent lifestyle.

## Main study material

Chapter 3

What did Jagan do for his son's meals?

A) He engaged a cook to prepare the meals

B) He cooked the meals himself

C) He sent his son to the college canteen for meals

D) He allowed his son to cook for himself

Answer: B

Explanation: After his wife's death, Jagan personally handled all cooking responsibilities for his son.

What was the dish that Jagan made for his son's breakfast?

A) Idli

B) Dosa

C) Uppumav

D) Vada

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan prepared uppumav for his son's breakfast, which was served on a plate and caught the morning sunlight.

What did Mai do after telling Jagan he didn't want to study anymore?

A) He went to his room and locked the door

B) He left for college on his bicycle

C) He helped Jagan in the sweetmeat shop

D) He skipped his breakfast and left

Answer: B

Explanation: After expressing his disinterest in studies, Mai put on his yellow shirt, took his bicycle, and departed.

What did Jagan's cousin do when he first arrived?

A) He went straight to the kitchen to try the sweets

B) He sat down with Jagan to discuss his son's issues

C) He asked about Jagan's wife's health

D) He helped out in the sweetmeat shop

Answer: A

Explanation: Upon arrival, Jagan's cousin immediately went to taste the sweets as they came out of the frying pan.

What was the reason given by Jagan for not engaging a cook at home?

A) Food is similar to breathing, so one shouldn't engage a cook

B) He preferred to cook the meals himself

C) He didn't trust cooks to prepare the food properly

D) He couldn't afford to hire a cook

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan compared food to breathing, stating one doesn't hire someone to breathe for them.

What was the purpose of Jagan holding long conferences with his son about his diet?

A) To ensure his son was eating a healthy and balanced diet

B) To bond with his son and discuss his daily activities

C) To control and micromanage his son's eating habits

D) To prepare elaborate menus for his son's meals

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan's nightly conferences were a manifestation of his obsessive control over his son's eating habits.

What detail did Jagan provide about the cost of ingredients for his sweets?

A) Sugar costs one and thirty per kilogram

B) Flour and butter cost thrice as much as sugar

C) Nutmegs cost seventy paise each

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan detailed all these specific costs regarding his sweet-making ingredients.

What did Jagan cite as the per capita income in India?

A) Three annas

B) Eight annas

C) One rupee

D) Three rupees

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan referenced a per capita income of three annas when discussing affordability.

What reason did Jagan's son give for not showing his story to anyone?

A) He was still developing the story and didn't want feedback

B) He was worried his father wouldn't understand or approve

C) He wanted to keep it a surprise until the competition deadline

D) He felt his father was just examining him and didn't really care

Answer: A

Explanation: Mai insisted he wasn't the type to show unfinished work to anyone.

What details did Jagan's son share about the writing competition in Ananda Vikatan magazine?

A) They would pay 25,000 rupees for the best novel

B) The deadline to submit was September 30th

C) He needed to fill out a coupon in the magazine

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: Mai mentioned all these specific details about the writing competition requirements.

What was the rare type of brain tumor that Jagan's wife had?

A) Benign growth

B) Malignant growth

C) Inoperable tumor

D) The doctor didn't specify the type

Answer: B

Explanation: The doctor identified it as a rare, malignant brain tumor.

How did Jagan's son obtain the coupon from the Ananda Vikatan magazine?

A) He bought a copy of the magazine

B) He carefully cut it out from the library copy

C) He asked the librarian for the coupon

D) He downloaded it online

Answer: B

Explanation: Mai cut out the coupon from the library's copy using a blade, risking punishment.

What did Jagan tell the head cook about his son's writing?

A) He boasted about his son's genius and earning potential

B) He was humble and downplayed his son's writing ambitions

C) He asked the cook for advice on how to support his son

D) He was nervous about his son's unconventional path

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan proudly announced his son's writing plans and potential earnings to the head cook.

What did Mai say when Jagan told him he was going to speak to his college?

A) He looked up angrily

B) He smiled grimly

C) He kicked away his breakfast

D) He screamed at the mention of college

Answer: A

Explanation: Mai responded with an angry look when Jagan mentioned speaking to the college.

What did Jagan do when he saw the light on in his son's room?

A) He knocked on the door to check on him

B) He peeked through the keyhole

C) He waited until morning to speak to him

D) He left his son alone

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan observed his son through the keyhole when he noticed the light was on.

What did Mai have in his room?

A) A bed and a desk

B) Books and building blocks

C) A television and toys

D) A personal computer

Answer: B

Explanation: Mai's room contained books heaped on a round table and house-building blocks.

What did Jagan's son do with his class books when the cousin met him?

A) He carefully put them away

B) He tore the pages savagely

C) He gave them to the cousin

D) He left them at college

Answer: B

Explanation: Mai violently tore up his class books and ordered them to be burned in the kitchen fire.

What philosophy of living did Jagan say he hoped his son would emulate?

A) Accumulating wealth and luxury

B) Simple living and high thinking, as Gandhi taught

C) Following his father's business path

D) Pursuing creative writing

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan expressed hope that his son would follow Gandhi's teaching of simple living and high thinking.

When did Jagan hold conferences with his son about his diet?

A) In the morning before breakfast

B) In the afternoon after lunch

C) In the evening before dinner

D) At night before retiring

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan conducted these dietary discussions with his son each night before bedtime.

What was Mai's reaction when Jagan said he liked his "idea" of becoming a writer?

A) He was excited and grateful

B) He remained doubtful and suspicious

C) He explained his writing plans

D) He dismissed his father's interest

Answer: B

Explanation: Mai remained suspicious of his father's support, following him mutely with lingering misgivings.

What was Mali's room called?

A) The study room

B) The quiet room

C) The cool room

D) The private chamber

Answer: C

Explanation: Mali's windowless hall was historically known as the "cool room."

What did the doctor tell Jagan to do when he left?

A) Go back to be with his wife in her final hours

B) Publish his book on nature cure

C) Take Mali with him

D) Call another doctor

Answer: A

Explanation: The doctor instructed Jagan to return to his wife for her remaining hours and protect their son.

How did Mali react when he saw his father crying about his mother's condition?

A) He comforted his father

B) He watched with dismay and puzzlement

C) He ran out of the house

D) He blamed his father

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali observed his father's emotional breakdown from a distance with a mix of dismay and confusion.

What verse did Jagan's son compose after sending his books to the fire?

A) "Let us show gratitude to the Great Fire that consumes our horrid books"

B) "Down with the educational system"

C) "Burn the books, free the mind"

D) "Books are shackles, fire is liberation"

Answer: A

Explanation: Mali spontaneously composed a verse expressing gratitude to the fire for consuming his "horrid books."

What happened to create an invisible barrier between Jagan and his son?

A) Their disagreement about education

B) Jagan's reaction to his wife's illness

C) Their different views on writing

D) Their conflicting personalities

Answer: B

Explanation: The barrier formed after Jagan's emotional breakdown upon learning of his wife's condition.

What did Jagan say about using artificial essences in his sweets?

A) He would never use them while in charge

B) He would consider using them to increase profits

C) He used them sparingly when needed

D) He left the decision to his head cook

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan firmly stated he would never use artificial essences as long as he controlled the establishment.

What reason did Mai give for cutting the coupon from the library magazine?

A) To prove his cleverness to the librarian

B) Lack of money to buy the magazine

C) Fear the librarian wouldn't allow its use

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: Mai provided all these reasons for his actions with the library magazine.

What was Jagan's educational background?

A) He had a B.A. degree

B) Left college due to Gandhi's call

C) Failed B.A. multiple times as private candidate

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan's education included all these experiences, from multiple B.A. attempts to leaving college following Gandhi's call.

What did Jagan imagine his son would be doing as a writer?

A) Burning midnight oil with papers everywhere

B) Composing poems lasting thousands of years

C) Bursting into inspired song like Kalidasa

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan envisioned all these romantic notions of his son's potential writing career.

How did Mai describe his college friends?

A) They were supportive of his writing

B) They were ordinary people focused on degrees

C) They were misleading him

D) They were fellow writers

Answer: B

Explanation: Mai dismissed his college friends as ordinary individuals only concerned with obtaining degrees.

What was the condition of Mali's room?

A) A long hall with windows

B) A hall without ventilation

C) A room next to the kitchen

D) A small study near the entrance

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali's room was a long hall without any ventilation or windows.

What did Mai say about wanting to write something different?

A) He wanted to write novels instead of stories

B) He wanted to write something more profound

C) He wanted to shock his father

D) He wanted to write in a different language

Answer: B

Explanation: Mai expressed desire to write something different and more meaningful than his father's work.

What time did Jagan hold his dietary conferences with Mali?

A) Morning after breakfast

B) Afternoon before lunch

C) Evening after dinner

D) Night before retiring

Answer: D

Explanation: These conversations about Mali's diet occurred nightly before bedtime.

What did Jagan say about the per capita income and sweet purchases?

A) Most couldn't afford rice for two meals

B) Parents should provide money for sweets

C) He couldn't give free sweets as charity

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan mentioned all these points when discussing affordability of his sweets.

What was the deadline for the writing competition?

A) August 30th

B) September 30th

C) October 30th

D) November 30th

Answer: B

Explanation: The competition deadline was specifically September 30th.

What price did Jagan mention for nutmegs?

A) Fifty paise each

B) Sixty paise each

C) Seventy paise each

D) Eighty paise each

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan specifically mentioned nutmegs cost seventy paise each.

What did Mali do with the torn pages of his books?

A) Threw them in the trash

B) Burned them in the kitchen fire

C) Left them on his desk

D) Gave them to his father

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali had the torn pages burned in the kitchen fire.

When did the invisible barrier between Jagan and Mali begin?

A) After Mali quit college

B) After his mother's diagnosis

C) When Mali started writing

D) During Mali's childhood

Answer: B

Explanation: The barrier formed following the revelation of Mali's mother's terminal condition.

What was the prize money for the writing competition?

A) 15,000 rupees

B) 20,000 rupees

C) 25,000 rupees

D) 30,000 rupees

Answer: C

Explanation: The competition offered a prize of 25,000 rupees for the best novel.

What did Jagan say about engaging cooks in his sweetmeat shop?

A) They were specialists and technicians

B) The shop was like a factory

C) He didn't believe in engaging cooks

D) Both A and B

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan explained that his shop was like a factory and required specialists and technicians.

## Main study material

Chapter 4

How long had it been since Jagan and Mali's last conversation?

A) A week ago

B) A month ago

C) Three and a half months ago

D) Six months ago

Answer: C

Explanation: Their last conversation occurred three and a half months prior to the current events.

Where was Mali spending his days according to the cousin?

A) At the Ananda Bhavan restaurant

B) At the town's public library

C) At a writing workshop

D) At a friend's place

Answer: B

Explanation: The cousin informed Jagan that Mali was spending his time at the Town Public Library.

What connection existed between Jagan and Mali in their daily routine?

A) Mali would leave a five-rupee note on the hall table every morning

B) They would have meals together at home

C) They would discuss Mali's writing

D) They would go for walks together

Answer: A

Explanation: Their only connection was the five-rupee note Mali left on the hall table each morning.

What was Mali using at the public library?

A) Books for research

B) The library's typewriter

C) Study rooms

D) Computers

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali had been using the library's typewriter for his work.

According to the cousin, how did Mali plan to finance his trip to America?

A) He had saved up the money himself

B) He had borrowed money from the library

C) Jagan was going to provide the funds

D) The cousin's friend in the airline had helped him

Answer: A

Explanation: Mali had managed to find the money in the house, indicating he had saved it himself.

Who helped Mali with his travel arrangements to America?

A) Jagan's friend in the government

B) The librarian's brother in the airline company

C) Mali's own connections

D) Both B and C

Answer: B

Explanation: The librarian's brother, who worked at the airline company, had assisted Mali with the arrangements.

What method of travel did Jagan prefer for Mali's journey to America?

A) By airplane

B) By ship

C) By train

D) By car

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan wanted Mali to travel by steamer as he considered it safer than air travel.

What was Mali doing in Madras?

A) Getting American clothes made

B) Applying for a visa

C) Meeting with travel agents

D) Both A and B

Answer: A

Explanation: Mali was in Madras getting his American clothes made for the trip.

What was Jagan's view on the food habits in America?

A) They only eat vegetarian food

B) They eat beef and pork

C) They eat healthy food

D) They eat traditional Indian food

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan believed Americans primarily consumed beef and pork.

What did Jagan say about the drinking habits in America?

A) They drink only water

B) They drink only milk

C) They drink intoxicating drinks

D) They drink herbal beverages

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan believed Americans consumed intoxicating drinks instead of water or milk.

Where did Jagan keep his savings?

A) In the bank

B) In the loft

C) In the puja room

D) Under his bed

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan kept his cash savings in the loft of his house.

What did Jagan plan to do with his saved cash?

A) Give it to Mali for his trip

B) Invest it in his business

C) Transfer it to the puja room

D) Donate it to charity

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan planned to move the money to a casket behind the family Gods in the puja room.

What was Mali's reason for visiting the library regularly?

A) To read books

B) To use the typewriter

C) To meet friends

D) To study

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali was regularly using the library's typewriter.

What was Jagan's opinion about proper attire in foreign countries?

A) One should wear casual clothes

B) One must wear tie and shoes all day

C) Traditional Indian clothes are best

D) Clothes don't matter

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan believed one must wear tie and shoes from morning till night in foreign countries.

What did the cousin say about women in America?

A) They follow traditional values

B) They mix freely with men

C) They stay at home

D) They don't work

Answer: B

Explanation: The cousin described American women as mixing freely with men and marrying easily.

How did Jagan initially justify Mali's decision to end his education?

A) Mali wanted to learn in the school of life

B) Mali was not interested in studies

C) Mali wanted to start working

D) Mali was not performing well

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan rationalized that Mali wanted to educate himself in the "school of life."

What was Jagan's reaction to Mali's absence?

A) He was pleased

B) He was shocked and worried

C) He was indifferent

D) He was angry

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan was shocked to learn Mali had left without his knowledge or permission.

What did Mali do with the money he found in the house?

A) Saved it for emergencies

B) Used it for daily expenses

C) Almost paid for his air ticket

D) Invested it in business

Answer: C

Explanation: Mali had used the money he found to nearly complete payment for his air ticket.

How did Mali plan his trip to America?

A) With Jagan's help

B) Through a travel agent

C) Working out the details himself

D) With his friends' help

Answer: C

Explanation: Mali had meticulously planned all the details of his trip independently.

What was Jagan's main concern about Mali's air travel?

A) The cost

B) The safety

C) The duration

D) The comfort

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan was worried about safety and preferred Mali to travel by ship instead.

What did Jagan initially think about the public library?

A) It was an important institution

B) It was insignificant

C) It was helpful for students

D) It was well-managed

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan believed if the library was important, he would have known about it.

What was Mali's status regarding his book?

A) He had finished writing it

B) He was still writing it

C) He planned to write it in America

D) He had abandoned the project

Answer: C

Explanation: According to the cousin, Mali intended to write his book after reaching America.

What did Jagan say about Mali's access to money?

A) Mali never asked for money

B) Mali could have everything he wanted

C) Mali needed to earn his own money

D) Mali was wasteful with money

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan stated that Mali could have everything he wanted, as the money was all for him.

What was Mali's attitude towards telling Jagan about his plans?

A) He was open and communicative

B) He kept everything secret

C) He discussed some aspects

D) He asked for advice

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali had kept his plans secret, not informing Jagan about his preparations or movements.

What did Jagan think about Mali's independence in planning?

A) Pure admiration

B) Complete disapproval

C) Mixed admiration and concern

D) Total indifference

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan felt both admiration for Mali's self-reliance and concern about his secretive actions.

What was the cousin's attitude towards Mali's plans?

A) Supportive of Mali

B) Supportive of Jagan

C) Neutral observer

D) Critical of both

Answer: A

Explanation: The cousin seemed to favor Mali's independence and enjoyed Jagan's predicament.

What did Jagan say about proper behavior in foreign countries?

A) One should follow local customs

B) One should maintain Indian traditions

C) One must wear formal attire always

D) One should adapt gradually

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan emphasized the importance of wearing formal attire like ties and shoes all day in foreign countries.

How did Mali obtain funds for his trip?

A) Through bank loans

B) From Jagan directly

C) By finding money in the house

D) From his savings

Answer: C

Explanation: Mali had found and used money kept in the house to fund his travel plans.

What was Mali's approach to his travel preparations?

A) Casual and unplanned

B) Detailed and methodical

C) Dependent on others

D) Limited and basic

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali had worked out all the details of his trip meticulously and independently.

What was Jagan's primary concern about Mali's American education?

A) The cost

B) The distance

C) The cultural differences

D) The educational quality

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan was most worried about American cultural practices and their influence on Mali.

## Main study material

Chapter 5

How did Jagan react when he received his son's airmail letter?

B) With indifference

Explanation: Jagan experienced intense joy upon receiving the airmail letter, comparable to his son's physical return.

How long did Jagan spend reading and re-reading his son's letter?

A) A few minutes

B) Nearly an hour

C) The whole morning

D) The entire day

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan sat on the hall bench scanning every word of Mali's letter for nearly an hour.

Who wrote the letters signed "GM" from America?

A) Mali

B) Jagan

C) Grace

D) Mali and Grace together

Answer: C

Explanation: Grace revealed that she had composed all the letters signed "GM" (Grace and Mali).

What was Grace's educational background?

A) Literature from Margaret's

B) Domestic science from Michigan

C) Creative writing from New Jersey

D) Art from Korea

Answer: B

Explanation: Grace studied domestic science at Michigan University.

Where did Grace meet Mali?

A) At a football game in Michigan

B) In a creative writing class

C) At a party in New Jersey

D) Through mutual friends

Answer: A

Explanation: Grace met Mali at a football game when he came to Michigan for his creative writing course.

What did Mali give Jagan as a gift from America?

B) A pale yellow casket with cutlery

C) A gold-plated wristwatch

D) A leather briefcase

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali and Grace gave Jagan a pale yellow casket containing spoons, forks, and knives.

What did Mali wear at home after returning from America?

A) Traditional Indian attire

B) Dark trousers and white shirt with slippers

C) Formal suits and ties

D) Casual jeans and t-shirts

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali wore dark trousers over a white shirt and always had his feet in slippers at home.

What did Grace do that surprised Jagan one morning?

B) Tidied his quarters

C) Asked to learn prayers

D) Organized a party

Explanation: Grace surprised Jagan by parting the curtain dividing the house and tidying his quarters.

How did Grace transform the hall?

B) With curtains, mats, tablecloth, and modern paintings

C) By expanding the space

D) With new lighting

Answer: B

Explanation: Grace had added curtains, mats, tablecloth, and modern paintings to transform the hall.

What facility did Mali suddenly request for the house?

B) Telephone

C) Car

D) Computer

Explanation: Mali suddenly asked Jagan to get a telephone installed in the house.

What practice did Jagan say he followed regarding food?

B) Eating only what he cooked himself

C) Following a strict diet

D) Fasting regularly

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan explained he was under a vow to eat only what he could cook with his own hands.

When did Mali usually go out of the house?

B) Afternoons

C) After darkness fell

D) Late at night

Explanation: Mali waited for darkness to descend before going out, dressed elaborately in formal attire.

What reason did Mali give for wanting a telephone?

A) To call relatives

B) To order groceries

C) To contact business associates

D) For emergencies

Explanation: Mali felt embarrassed that he couldn't give his associates a telephone number.

What type of letter from Mali did Jagan rigorously suppress?

B) About eating beef

D) About his request for money

Explanation: Jagan suppressed the letter where Mali wrote about taking to eating beef in America.

According to Grace, why was she initially afraid to come to India?

B) The food

D) The language barrier

Explanation: Grace admitted she had heard so much about the caste system that she was afraid to come to India.

What did Grace do in the household that made Jagan uneasy?

B) Rearranged furniture

C) Helped with cleaning and chores

D) Decorated with flowers

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan felt uneasy when Grace helped with household chores like cleaning vessels and sweeping.

How did Mali carry himself when out with Grace?

A) Like a common person

B) Like a celebrity avoiding attention

C) Very friendly and outgoing

D) Nervously and uncomfortably

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali carried himself like a celebrity avoiding the attention of the rabble when out with Grace.

What was Jagan's reaction to seeing modern paintings in his hall?

B) He found them bewildering

D) He was indifferent

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan found the modern paintings bewildering but agreed they were marvelous when Grace asked.

Where had Jagan kept Mali's letters from America?

B) In a cardboard box in his favorite cupboard

C) In Mali's room

D) At his shop

Answer: B

Explanation: The letters were stored neatly in a cardboard box in Jagan's favorite cupboard.

What did Grace do when Jagan mentioned being a very active man?

A) Ignored his comment

B) Drew a chair for him

C) Asked him to exercise

D) Changed the subject

Answer: B

Explanation: When Jagan mentioned being active, Grace drew a chair and asked him to be seated.

B) Lying

C) Killing a cow

D) Adultery

Answer: C

Explanation: Killing a cow headed the list of the five deadly sins according to the shastras.

How did Mali's appearance change since his arrival?

C) The foreignness was gone and he looked below par

Explanation: The freshness and glow of foreignness had faded, and Mali looked a shade below par.

What did Grace say about being an Indian daughter-in-law?

B) She must not forget her role

C) She enjoyed the experience

D) She needed to learn more

Answer: B

Explanation: Grace said she must not forget that she was an Indian daughter-in-law.

Who did Jagan first share the news of Mali's letter with?

B) The adjournment lawyer

C) Nataraj at Truth Printing Works

D) Random people on the street

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan first went to Truth Printing Works to share the news with Nataraj.

What did Grace say about Mali's decision to bring her to India?

B) It was necessary

C) It was very courageous

D) It was unexpected

Answer: C

Explanation: Grace said it was very courageous of Mali to bring her to India.

How did Mali explain to strangers?

C) Avoided meeting them

D) Asked Grace to entertain them

Answer: A

Explanation: Mali seated his visitors in the hall and conversed in low tones, as became a gentleman.

What was Grace's reaction to Jagan's natural food philosophy?

B) Said it was thrilling

C) Disagreed completely

D) Showed indifference

Answer: B

Explanation: Grace found Jagan's natural food philosophy thrilling, the first positive reaction he had received.

What did Grace say about Mali's communication with his father?

B) It needed improvement

C) Mali should have told him about his plans

D) It wasn't important

Answer: C

Explanation: Grace believed Mali should have told his father about his plans and education.

How did Jagan respond to questions about Mali's eating habits?

A) Discussed them openly

B) Said he didn't pry into others' food choices

C) Criticized Mali's choices

D) Asked Grace about them

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan said what another person eats or drinks never interests him and he doesn't pry.

What did Grace do with the household tasks?

D) Maintained close relationships

Answer: C

Explanation: Mali would seat his friends in the hall and converse in low, formal tones.

What was Grace's attitude toward household work?

A) Refused to participate

B) Hired help instead

C) Embraced it as an Indian daughter-in-law

D) Delegated to others

Answer: C

Explanation: Grace expressed that she must not forget her role as an Indian daughter-in-law.

What did the prospectus claim about the machine's impact?

A) Economic benefits only

B) Cultural advancement

C) Technical innovation

D) Job creation

Answer: B

Explanation: The prospectus emphasized the machine's role in cultural advancement and progress.

What was Jagan's final decision about the business proposition?

A) Full support

B) Partial investment

C) Peaceful non-cooperation

D) Direct refusal

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan chose to follow Gandhi's teaching of peaceful non-cooperation in response to Mali's demands.

Chapter 7

What did the placard at the shop entrance announce?

A) A new product launch

B) A discount on all items

C) A fixed price for any packet

D) A change in business hours

Answer: C

Explanation: The placard announced "Any packet 25 paise," setting a fixed price for all sweet packets.

How long did the shop's daily stock of sweets last?

A) Until the end of the day

B) Only a few hours

C) Until noon

D) It was never depleted

Answer: B

Explanation: The entire stock of sweets was exhausted by five o'clock, lasting only a few hours.

What did Jagan instruct the cooks to do after the sweets were sold out?

A) Prepare more sweets

B) Go home for the day

C) Assist with crowd control

D) Clean the kitchen

Answer: B

Explanation: When the cooks asked what to do after the sweets were sold out, Jagan told them to go home.

What was the bearded man's profession?

A) Temple builder

B) Hair dye specialist

C) Image maker

D) Sculptor

Answer: B

Explanation: The bearded man was a "blackener of white hair" who specialized in hair dyeing services.

What did the bearded man say about the "master's" artwork in temples?

A) It was limited to local temples

B) It was present throughout the South

C) It was only in ancient temples

D) It was commissioned rarely

Answer: B

Explanation: The master's artwork, including figures and designs, was present in temples throughout the South.

How did customers react to the new pricing?

A) They were indifferent

B) They were enthusiastic

C) They were skeptical

D) They were angry

Answer: B

Explanation: Customers showed great enthusiasm, with people falling over each other to buy the sweets.

What was Sivaraman's initial reaction to the pricing change?

A) Confusion and uncertainty

B) Excitement and support

C) Indifference and apathy

D) Anger and frustration

Answer: A

Explanation: Sivaraman expressed confusion, saying "I do not understand" and questioning the purpose of the change.

What did Sivaraman's assistant express concern about?

A) Quality of sweets

B) Being overwhelmed by demand

C) Low prices

D) Staff reduction

Answer: B

Explanation: The assistant worried they would be swamped by the increased demand.

How did Jagan's staff fear the new pricing would affect them?

A) Job security concerns

B) Reduced wages

C) Increased workload

D) Changed work hours

Answer: A

Explanation: The staff feared the pricing strategy would affect their job security and career prospects.

What solution did Sivaraman suggest for the increased demand?

A) Increasing production

B) Hiring more staff

C) Extending hours

D) Raising prices

Answer: A

Explanation: Sivaraman suggested they might need to prepare more sweets to meet demand.

What did Jagan claim was the purpose of the new pricing?

A) To increase profits

B) To allow more people to eat sweets

C) To compete with others

D) To reduce inventory

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan stated his purpose was to let more people enjoy eating sweets.

What did Jagan offer to do with the staff's newfound leisure time?

A) Teach cooking techniques

B) Share business strategies

C) Read the Bhagavad Gita

D) Conduct training sessions

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan offered to read and discuss the Bhagavad Gita with his staff during their free time.

What did the bearded man say about his relationship with the "master"?

A) He was the master's son

B) He was the closest disciple

C) He was a business partner

D) He was a casual student

Answer: B

Explanation: The bearded man was the master's closest disciple, being the only one allowed near him in his final years.

Where was the "master's" home located?

A) Near Jagan's shop

B) Across the river

C) In the city center

D) Next to the temple

Answer: B

Explanation: The master's home was on the other bank of the river, with its trees visible from Jagan's side.

What was Jagan's routine for years?

A) Going between statue and shop

B) Visiting temples daily

C) Working at multiple locations

D) Traveling around the city

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan's fixed routine had been going between the statue and his shop.

How did the bearded man respond to Jagan mentioning Gandhi as his master?

A) With interest

B) With jealousy

C) With respect

D) With indifference

Answer: B

Explanation: The bearded man showed no interest, possibly jealous of the term "master" being applied to anyone else.

What did Jagan remember about his experiences near the river?

A) Sunset colors only

B) Bird sounds only

C) Gandhi's assemblies only

D) All of these experiences

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan remembered the sunset colors, bird sounds in Nallappa's Grove, and Gandhi's assemblies on the river sands.

Who was the bearded man's best customer?

A) The salt

B) Jagan

C) The temple builder

D) The master

Answer: A

Explanation: The salt was the bearded man's best customer for hair dyeing services.

What did the bearded man say about the master's home decoration?

A) It was full of artwork

B) It had no artwork

C) It was elaborately decorated

D) It was under renovation

Answer: B

Explanation: The master was not the type to keep his handiwork in his own house.

How did Jagan initially respond to the hair dye offer?

A) Immediate acceptance

B) Hesitant consideration

C) Direct refusal

D) Indifference

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan hesitated, considering what Mali and Grace might say about it.

What expertise did Jagan claim about hair color?

A) He had written a book about it

B) He was a practicing specialist

C) He had studied it abroad

D) He had no expertise

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan remembered he had written a book on the subject that would be published someday.

What did the bearded man say about bears and grey hair?

A) Bears never have grey hair

B) Bears age differently

C) Bears dye their hair

D) Bears live longer

Answer: A

Explanation: The bearded man agreed with Jagan's joke, saying that's why bears never have grey hair.

How did Jagan respond to the invitation to visit the master's home?

A) Immediate acceptance

B) Polite refusal

C) Requested time to think

D) Made excuses

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan promptly said "Tomorrow" when asked when he would visit.

What did the crowd outside do after the sweets were sold out?

A) Left quietly

B) Continued waiting

C) Demanded refunds

D) Became aggressive

Answer: B

Explanation: The crowd remained outside waiting, even after being told everything was sold out.

How did the cousin respond to the cooks' questions?

A) Gave immediate answers

B) Promised explanations later

C) Ignored their concerns

D) Consulted with Jagan

Answer: B

Explanation: The cousin promised to explain everything to them tomorrow.

What did the bearded man say about temple builders?

A) They rarely commissioned work

B) They regularly sought the master

C) They built without artwork

D) They worked independently

Answer: B

Explanation: The master was regularly besieged by temple builders seeking his work.

How did Jagan view his current routine compared to his past?

A) Preferred current routine

B) Felt constrained now

C) Saw no difference

D) Enjoyed both equally

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan sighed at the reminder of his current fixed routine, suggesting he felt constrained.

What did Jagan say about his temple visits?

A) Visited daily

B) Never visited

C) Hadn't visited in months

D) Planned future visits

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan admitted he hadn't gone near a temple for months due to being wrapped up in work.

What did the bearded man carry in the salt's car?

A) Hair dye supplies

B) Temple artwork

C) Business documents

D) Personal belongings

Answer: A

Explanation: The bearded man traveled in the salt's car to provide hair dyeing services.

How did Jagan's staff view him as a businessman?

A) As incompetent

B) As astute

C) As unpredictable

D) As generous

Answer: B

Explanation: The staff viewed Jagan as an astute businessman and credited him with having strategic purposes.

## Main study material

Chapter 8

What was covering the pond?

B) Water lilies

D) Duckweed

What type of trees were looming over the small shrine?

C) Palm, coconut, and eucalyptus

D) Cedar, fir, and spruce

Answer: B

Explanation: Banyan, peepul, and mango trees loomed over the small shrine.

What vegetation was intertwined around the surroundings?

B) Vines, moss, and ferns

C) Weeds and wildflowers

D) Grass and shrubs

What did the bearded man say his master lived his whole life doing?

B) Carving images

C) Tending to the garden

D) Meditating in the sanctum

What was the bearded man's age?

B) 60 years old

Explanation: The bearded man explicitly stated he was sixty-nine years old.

Where did the bearded man say his master worked on ornamental details?

B) In the pillared hall

C) In the backyard

D) In the sanctum

Explanation: The master worked on ornamental details in a corner of the pillared hall.

What did the bearded man say happened when his master died?

B) He cremated the body beyond the pond

D) He carried the body to the city

Explanation: The bearded man cremated his master's body beyond the pond.

What did the bearded man say his master had dreamed of creating?

B) A dancing figure of Nataraj

C) A new God for the temple

D) Both A and C

Answer: D

Explanation: The master dreamed of creating both a five-faced Gayatri deity and a new God for the temple.

What was the bearded man's profession on Kabir Street?

A) Temple builder

B) Hair dye specialist

C) Image maker

D) Sculptor

Answer: B

Explanation: The bearded man worked as a hair dye specialist on Kabir Street.

How did the bearded man say the master ensured images wouldn't be too perfect?

B) He chipped off a piece

C) He placed them in protective cases

D) He damaged them purposely

Answer: A

Explanation: They always created a small flaw in every image for safety.

What did the bearded man say about the stone's water-seasoning?

B) It would bring out surface grains

C) It required running water

D) It was a common practice

Answer: B

Explanation: The water treatment was needed to bring out the stone's surface grains.

What was Jagan's reaction when seeing the lines on the stone?

A) He saw intricate designs

B) He noticed subtle markings

C) He saw no difference from other stones

D) He admired the craftsmanship

Explanation: To Jagan's eyes, the stone appeared no different from any other block of stone.

How many arms did the bearded man say the Goddess would have?

B) Eight arms

D) Twelve arms

Explanation: The Goddess was described as having ten arms holding various divine objects.

What did the bearded man say about people's fear of visiting the place?

B) Fear of wild growth

C) Fear of snakes

D) Fear of spirits

Explanation: People were afraid to visit because of snakes, but the master loved them.

What did the bearded man's master say about sharing fruits with monkeys?

B) The fruits belonged to humans

C) We must not monopolize the earth

D) The monkeys were destructive

Answer: C

Explanation: The master said they must not monopolize the earth and would share fruits with the monkeys.

What did Jagan do when they reached the top of the steps with the stone?

B) Asked for help

C) Collapsed on the grass

D) Continued carrying it

Answer: C

Explanation: Upon reaching the top, Jagan let go of the stone and collapsed on the grass.

What did the bearded man say about his plans if he completed the Goddess image?

B) Train apprentices

C) Die peacefully on his 70th birthday

D) Start a new project

Explanation: He said he would die peacefully if he could complete this task.

What did the bearded man suggest Jagan should do with the garden?

B) Buy it and install the Goddess

C) Turn it into a temple

D) Leave it as is

Answer: B

Explanation: The bearded man suggested Jagan buy the garden and install the Goddess.

How did the bearded man's status change in the garden compared to Kabir Street?

A) More humble

B) More authoritarian

C) More friendly

D) More distant

Answer: B

Explanation: He transformed from a hair-blackener to a commanding leader in the garden setting.

What did the master say about clearing the wild growth?

A) It should be done regularly

B) It was unnecessary

C) It helped prevent snakes

D) It improved accessibility

Answer: B

Explanation: The master never approved of clearing the wild growth around the area.

What happened to the God in the temple's sanctum?

B) It was stolen

C) It was relocated

D) It deteriorated

Answer: B

Explanation: The God in the sanctum had been stolen years before they arrived.

What did Jagan refuse to eat when offered?

A) The guava fruit

B) A sweet

C) A mango

D) A rice cake

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan refused to eat the guava fruit as he kept off sweets and salt.

What did the bearded man say about his customer, the salt?

A) He was wealthy

B) He was his best customer

C) He was difficult

D) He was generous

Answer: B

Explanation: The salt was described as the bearded man's best customer for hair dyeing.

What did the bearded man say about the stone's size?

A) Two feet square

B) Three feet square

C) Four feet square

D) Five feet square

Answer: A

Explanation: The stone was described as being a two-foot-square block.

What did the bearded man say about cracks in the stone?

A) They were inevitable

B) They could be repaired

C) It was in God's hands

D) They were preventable

Answer: C

Explanation: He said whether the stone would crack was in God's hands.

How did the birds and frogs react to the bearded man's singing?

A) They joined in harmony

B) They fled and scattered

C) They remained still

D) They approached closer

Answer: B

Explanation: The birds fluttered and frogs sprang back into the water when he burst into song.

What did the bearded man say about his master's possessions?

A) They filled many rooms

B) They fit in one's palm

C) They were valuable

D) They were lost

Answer: B

Explanation: All the master possessed could be contained within the palm of one's hand.

What did Jagan think about his own life compared to the master's?

A) It was more fulfilling

B) It was more successful

C) It was more narrow

D) It was more spiritual

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan realized how narrow his existence had been between the statue and shop.

What did the bearded man say about the scriptural guidance for couples?

A) They should live separately

B) They should vanish into the forest

C) They should stay in cities

D) They should travel widely

Answer: B

Explanation: According to scriptures, husband and wife must vanish into the forest at some stage.

What did the bearded man say about the Goddess's heads?

A) They were all identical

B) They had different colors

C) They faced different directions

D) They represented seasons

Answer: B

Explanation: The Goddess combined all colors in her five heads of different colors.

Chapter 9

What was the name of the protagonist?

A) Nataraj

B) Jagan

C) Mali

D) Sivaraman

Answer: B

Explanation: The protagonist's name is Jagan.

What did Jagan do to cool himself down?

A) Took a cold shower

B) Drank cold water

C) Fanned himself with a cloth

D) Poured cold water over himself

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan poured cold water over himself after coming from the back yard.

What was Jagan doing when his son Mali came to see him?

A) Cooking dinner

B) Spinning cotton

C) Praying to the Gods

D) Reading a book

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan was sitting in the courtyard, turning the spinning wheel and drawing a fine thread.

What was the purpose of Jagan's book that Nataraj had not published?

A) To establish the science of food-making

B) To promote the use of hair dye

C) To describe the process of spinning cotton

D) To advocate for economic reforms

Answer: A

Explanation: Jagan was trying to establish the importance of precision in food-making in his book.

What was Jagan's initial reaction when Mali came to see him?

A) He was excited and welcomed him warmly

B) He quickly covered his bare chest with a towel

C) He ignored Mali and continued spinning

D) He scolded Mali for interrupting his work

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan quickly snatched a towel and hid his chest under it when Mali arrived.

What was Jagan's profession?

A) Sculptor

B) Tailor

C) Sweets maker

D) Farmer

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan was the maker of sweets and gatherer of money each day.

What was the purpose of the bearded man's visit to Jagan's shop?

A) To buy sweets

B) To install an image of the Goddess

C) To recruit Jagan as a sculptor

D) To sell hair dye products

Answer: B

Explanation: The bearded man came to get help for installing the image of the Goddess.

What was Jagan's approach to cooking food?

A) He was indifferent to precision and timing

B) He believed food-making was an art, not a science

C) He preferred to cook food quickly for maximum nutrition

D) He believed precision and timing were crucial for proper cooking

Answer: D

Explanation: Jagan believed that in food-making, precision was crucial and it was a science.

What did Jagan use to light the oil wick in his house?

A) Matches

B) A flint and steel

C) A lighter

D) Not specified in the text

Answer: D

Explanation: The text does not mention the specific method Jagan used to light the oil wick.

How did Jagan feel about the sound of his spinning wheel?

A) It annoyed him and he wanted to stop it

B) It was a distraction that prevented him from thinking

C) It was a comforting and soothing sound

D) He was indifferent to the noise it made

Answer: C

Explanation: The slight whirring noise of the wheel was very comforting and stilling to the nerves and thoughts for Jagan.

What was the purpose of the low-wattage light bulbs in Jagan's house?

A) To save electricity

B) To create a cozy and intimate atmosphere

C) To benefit the human retina

D) To discourage unwanted visitors

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan had fitted only ten-watt bulbs to benefit the human retina.

What was Jagan's relationship with his printer, Nataraj?

A) They were close friends

B) Nataraj was indifferent to Jagan's ideas

C) Nataraj actively opposed Jagan's plans

D) Nataraj was Jagan's business partner

Answer: B

Explanation: Nataraj was indifferent to the task of publishing Jagan's book.

What was Jagan's opinion on the monsoon that year?

A) He believed it would be delayed

B) He thought it would arrive earlier than usual

C) He was unsure about the timing of the monsoon

D) He did not have any specific thoughts about the monsoon

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan reflected that perhaps the monsoon would be breaking earlier that year.

What was the significance of the coconut trees mentioned in the passage?

A) They provided shade and shelter for Jagan

B) They were important for the local economy

C) They represented the natural setting and environment

D) They were a source of food and raw materials

Answer: C

Explanation: The text describes the sky-reaching coconut trees as part of the natural surroundings.

Why did Mali want brighter light in the room?

A) To read the cable he had received

B) To better see Jagan's facial expressions

C) To create a more comfortable atmosphere

D) To help Jagan with his spinning work

Answer: A

Explanation: Mali asked for brighter light to read the cable he had received.

What was the purpose of the cable that Mali's "associates" had sent him?

A) To inquire about the status of their project

B) To request Mali to return to the United States

C) To inform Mali about a change in their business plans

D) To ask Mali to provide more details about their venture

Answer: A

Explanation: The cable asked about the status of their project.

How did Mali respond when Jagan offered to leave the sweet shop business to him?

A) He was excited and grateful for the opportunity

B) He made a wry face and expressed his disinterest

C) He accepted it reluctantly, knowing it was his duty

D) He ignored the offer and continued discussing other matters

Answer: B

Explanation: The boy made a wry face at the mention of the shop when Jagan offered to leave it in his charge.

What was Mali's opinion about the value of Jagan's sweet business?

A) He believed it was a thriving and profitable business

B) He thought it was a worthwhile venture worth keeping

C) He considered it to be a declining and insignificant business

D) He was unsure about the current state and future prospects

Answer: C

Explanation: Mali said "In any case your business is worth nothing now."

Why did Jagan want to talk to Grace?

A) To discuss Mali's business plans with her

B) To understand if she was unhappy living in their house

C) To convince her to stay and continue her household duties

D) To find out where she had gone at that late hour of the night

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan wanted to understand if she was thinking of leaving and if anything had made her unhappy.

What was Mali's response when Jagan asked about Grace's whereabouts?

A) He openly shared that Grace had gone out for a walk

B) He refused to disclose where Grace had gone, claiming she was free

C) He insisted that Grace had left because she had no work to do at home

D) He assured Jagan that Grace would return soon

Answer: B

Explanation: When Jagan asked about Grace's whereabouts, Mali simply said "She has gone out," refusing to provide details.

How did Mali respond to Jagan's traditional views on marriage?

A) He agreed with Jagan and promised to keep Grace at home

B) He challenged Jagan's views, stating "That was in your day"

C) He ignored Jagan's comments

D) He acknowledged Jagan's concerns

Answer: B

Explanation: When Jagan said a wife must be with her husband, Mali replied, "That was in your day."

What was the purpose of Grace's presence in Jagan's household?

A) To assist Jagan with his sweet-making business

B) To provide companionship and care for the family

C) To work on Mali's business project as mentioned in the notice

D) To manage the household responsibilities

Answer: C

Explanation: Grace had come to work with Mali, as indicated by her name in the notice.

What was Mali's opinion on the value of the knowledge he had gained in the United States?

A) He believed it was a waste of money

B) He felt it was a valuable asset that could benefit the country

C) He was unsure about how to apply his knowledge

D) He thought it was irrelevant to the family's business

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali said he had learned valuable things in the United States that could benefit the country.

How did Jagan react when Mali described his "fiction-writing machine"?

A) He was impressed and interested in the concept

B) He was skeptical and questioned the feasibility

C) He listened attentively without expressing any opinion

D) He dismissed it as impractical

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan listened while the stars ran their course as Mali described his fiction-writing machine.

How did Jagan feel about the presence of Grace in his household?

A) He was indifferent to her presence

B) He was annoyed and wanted her to leave

C) He liked her presence and felt it filled an important void

D) He was suspicious of her motives

Answer: C

Explanation: Jagan liked her presence in the house, which filled a serious lacuna.

How did Mali describe the state of Jagan's sweet business?

A) He praised it as a thriving enterprise

B) He dismissed it as a worthless business

C) He acknowledged its value but showed no interest

D) He expressed uncertainty about its state

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali directly told Jagan "In any case your business is worth nothing now."

What was Jagan's response when Mali accused him of being the "talk of the town"?

A) He denied the accusation

B) He brushed it off and said "Let them"

C) He asked for more details

D) He promised to change his behavior

Answer: B

Explanation: Jagan did not wish to pursue the subject and mumbled, "Let them."

What was the purpose of the spinning wheel in Jagan's possession?

A) It was a tool for his book on food-making

B) It was a hobby and source of relaxation

C) It was a means to earn extra income

D) It was a way to follow Gandhi's advice

Answer: B

Explanation: The spinning wheel provided comfort and helped still Jagan's nerves and thoughts.

What did Mali claim about his studies in the United States?

A) They were a waste of money

B) They cost several thousand dollars

C) They were incomplete

D) They were irrelevant to India

Answer: B

Explanation: Mali mentioned he had learned valuable things in the United States at a cost of several thousand dollars.

What was Jagan's initial reaction to the bearded man's description of the Goddess?

A) He was confused and skeptical

B) He was intrigued and found it thrilling

C) He was offended

D) He was indifferent

Chapter 10

What did Mali tell the cousin about Grace?

B) Grace was going back to America to settle personal matters

C) Grace was going back to America to visit her family

D) Grace was going back to America to get married

Answer: A

Explanation: Mali told the cousin that Grace was going back to America soon on business, related to his machine.

What did the cousin say about the Ananda Bhavan sait?

B) The Ananda Bhavan sait was planning to buy shares in Mali's company

C) The Ananda Bhavan sait was upset with Jagan's decision to increase prices

Answer: D

How did the cousin describe the trip to the Tirupathi Hills?

B) It was a tiring trip, and the cousin had to accompany them against his will

C) It was an expensive trip, and the cousin could not afford to be away for so long

D) It was a family affair, and the cousin was not allowed to leave until it was over

Answer: A

Explanation: The cousin described it as a grand trip, where they had engaged three cottages and the whole temple was open to them.

What did the cousin say about Mali's relationship with him?
