Q7: What aspects of nature affect Maya deeply?

A) Colors and the call of birds

B) Storms and earthquakes

C) Oceans and mountains

D) Deserts and cacti

Answer: A

Ex- The text states that Maya is affected deeply by colors and by nature, especially the call of birds, such as the peacock.

Q8: What does Maya's sensitivity lead to?

A) Artistic talent

B) Psychosis and total madness

C) Spiritual enlightenment

D) Scientific curiosity

Answer: B

Ex- The text mentions that Maya's sensitivity leads her to the point of psychosis, and later total madness.

Q9: What does Maya become in the end, haunted by the prediction and all alone?

A) A nun

B) A recluse

C) A murderess

D) A poet

Answer: C

Ex- The text states that in the end, haunted by the prediction and all alone, Maya becomes a murderess.

Q10: As a young girl, where does Maya often play with her widowed father?

A) In the garden

B) In the library

C) In the kitchen

D) In the attic

Answer: A

Ex- The text mentions that as a young girl, Maya plays in the garden, often with her widowed father.

Q11: What does Maya's imagination allow her to do?

A) Turn objects into playthings and ordinary sadness into nightmares

B) Communicate with spirits

C) Predict the future

D) Fly like a bird

Answer: A

Ex- The text states that Maya has the imagination to turn objects into playthings and ordinary sadness into nightmares.

Q12: After her marriage, where does Maya spend most of her time?

A) At her husband's office

B) At her friend's house

C) At home, inside or in the garden

D) Traveling the world

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions that after her marriage, Maya broods most of the time at home, inside or in the garden.

Q13: What is Gautama's profession?

A) Doctor

B) Teacher

C) Lawyer

D) Businessman

Answer: C

Ex- The text describes Gautama as a lawyer who marries Maya.

Q14: How does Gautama respond to Maya's challenge about only feeling 'bored' at a party?

A) He apologizes for his lack of emotion

B) He defends himself by saying that boredom was just a fact

C) He accuses her of being too emotional

D) He ignores her comment

Answer: B

Ex- The text states that when Maya challenges Gautama for only feeling 'bored' at a party, he defends himself by saying that the boredom was just a fact.

Q15: What is Gautama's attitude towards facts?

A) Facts are made to be accepted and studied, not to be wept over

B) Facts are irrelevant in the face of emotions

C) Facts are meant to be challenged and questioned

D) Facts are less important than intuition

Answer: A

Ex- Gautama says, 'Facts are made to be accepted and to be studied. Not to be wept over.'

Q16: How does Gautama show kindness to Maya?

A) By giving her expensive gifts

B) By taking care of her when she is ill

C) By spending all his free time with her

D) By encouraging her to pursue her hobbies

Answer: B

Ex- The text mentions that Gautama takes care of Maya when she is ill, has headaches, or lies in bed with some kind of 'female ailment.'

Q17: Despite his kindness, how does Gautama treat Maya?

A) He is supportive and understanding

B) He is indifferent and uncaring

C) He is censorious and constantly tells her to 'sit up' or 'change your sari'

D) He is abusive and violent

Answer: C

Ex- The text states that despite his superficial kindness, Gautama is censorious and constantly tells Maya to 'sit up' or 'change your sari.'

Q18: Where does Gautama spend long hours working?

A) In his office in the city and at home

B) In the garden

C) At the library

D) At his friend's house

Answer: A

Ex- The text mentions that Gautama spends long hours in his office, not only the one in the city but also the one in his home.

Q19: What does Gautama read besides case law?

A) Poetry

B) Novels

C) Religious texts, like the Bhagavad Gita

D) Scientific journals

Answer: C

Ex- The text states that Gautama also reads religious texts, like the Bhagavad Gita, in addition to case law.

Q20: How does Gautama respond when Maya suggests going on a holiday?

A) He agrees enthusiastically

B) He says it's unlikely because he's too busy with work

C) He says they can't afford it

D) He ignores her suggestion

Answer: B

Ex- When Maya suggests going on a holiday, Gautama says, 'Unlikely,' snapping open his cigarette case with a metallic click that matched his voice.

Q21: What do Gautama's family members talk about in order to relax?

A) Love and affection

B) Politics and government

C) Art and literature

D) Their personal lives

Answer: B

Ex- The text mentions that in order to relax, Gautama's family members sometimes play cards, but they play so intensely that they find they have to release the mounting pressure by conversing and begin to talk again of politics and government.

Q22: How does Gautama handle the situation when Maya's pet dog dies?

A) He comforts Maya and grieves with her

B) He scolds Maya for being too attached to the dog

C) He does all that has to be done, quickly and quietly like a surgeon's knife at work

D) He ignores the situation and leaves Maya to deal with it alone

Answer: C

Ex- The text states that when Maya's pet dog dies, Gautama does all that has to be done, quickly and quietly like a surgeon's knife at work.

Q23: What is Rai Sahib's profession?

A) Doctor

B) Businessman

C) Retired lawyer

D) Politician

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions that Rai Sahib is a retired lawyer.

Q24: Where was Rai Sahib educated?

A) India

B) England

C) America

D) France

Answer: B

Ex- The text states that Rai Sahib was educated in England.

Q25: What did Rai Sahib do during the fight against the British for Independence?

A) He fought on the side of the British

B) He remained neutral

C) He spent time in prison

D) He fled the country

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions that Rai Sahib spent time in prison during the fight against the British for Independence.

Q26: How is Rai Sahib portrayed in the novel?

A) As a well-rounded character with a lot of depth

B) As 'the father', without much depth of character

C) As a minor character with little significance to the plot

D) As the main antagonist

Answer: B

Ex- The text states that Rai Sahib is portrayed as 'the father', without much depth of character, because that is how he is perceived by Maya, whose disturbed psychology is at the heart of this searing drama.

Q27: What does Rai Sahib love as a result of his legal training?

A) Chaos and unpredictability

B) Spontaneity and flexibility

C) Order, patterns, habits, and repetitions

D) Change and novelty

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions that given his legal training, it is not surprising that Rai Sahib is a very disciplined man, who loves order, patterns, habits, and repetitions.

Q28: How does Rai Sahib's affection for Maya come across?

A) As the sweet attention of an older father

B) As cold and distant

C) As manipulative and controlling

D) As inappropriate and disturbing

Answer: A

Ex- The text states that Rai Sahib's affection can come across as the sweet attention of an older father.

Q29: What does Rai Sahib do every day in his retirement?

A) He travels the world

B) He writes his memoirs

C) He maintains and inspects his garden

D) He volunteers at a local charity

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions that Rai Sahib spends a lot of time maintaining his garden, which becomes his arena for discipline in his retirement. He inspects it every day.

Q30: How does Rai Sahib react when a woman with loose, straggling hair arrives sobbing in his garden?

A) He comforts her and offers her shelter

B) He ignores her and continues with his breakfast

C) He is appalled at how human beings can be 'a magnet for disorder and failure'

D) He asks her to leave immediately

Answer: C

Ex- After the sobbing woman leaves, Rai Sahib explains to Maya that he is appalled at how human beings can be 'a magnet for disorder and failure.'

Part I

Q1: What lay rotting in the sun all day?

A) A dead branch

B) A dead animal

C) A dead human body

D) A dead bird

Answer: C

Ex- The text states, "All day the body lay rotting in the sun."

Q2: Why couldn't the body be moved onto the verandah?

A) It was too heavy to move

B) The reek of dead flesh was overpowering in the April heat

C) The verandah was too small

D) The family wanted it to remain in the sun

Answer: B

Ex- The text mentions, "It could not be moved onto the verandah for in that April heat, the reek of dead flesh was overpowering."

Q3: Where was the string bed with the body moved to?

A) Under the mango trees

B) Under the banyan tree

C) Under the lime trees

D) Under the neem trees

Answer: C

Ex- The text states, "So she moved the little string bed on which it lay under the lime trees."

Q4: What did Maya do when she saw the corpse's eyes open and staring?

A) She fainted

B) She called for help

C) She screamed and rushed to the garden tap to wash the vision from her eyes

D) She sat down and cried

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions, "saw its eyes open and staring still, screamed and rushed to the garden tap to wash the vision from her eyes."

Q5: Who drew the corpse's eyelids down?

A) Maya

B) Maya's husband

C) The gardener

D) The sweeper

Answer: C

Ex- The text states, "The gardener came and drew its eyelids down with two horny thumbs."

Q6: Who was supposed to bury the corpse?

A) The gardener

B) Maya

C) Maya's husband

D) The sweeper

Answer: D

Ex- The text mentions, "Often in the course of that day he said to her patiently, 'The sweeper will do it.'"

Q7: Where had the sweeper gone?

A) To the market

B) To visit his brother

C) To the temple

D) To another village

Answer: B

Ex- The text states, "He has gone to visit his brother but when he returns he will do it."

Q8: What birds sat in a circle around the corpse?

A) Vultures

B) Hawks

C) Crows

D) Eagles

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions, "Crows sat in a circle around the corpse."

Q9: What insects began to hum amidst the limes?

A) Mosquitoes

B) Flies

C) Bees

D) Butterflies

Answer: B

Ex- The text states, "Flies began to hum amidst the limes."

Q10: Who sent his wife to take Maya into the house?

A) The sweeper

B) The gardener

C) Maya's husband

D) The visitor

Answer: B

Ex- The text mentions, "The gardener sent his wife to take her into the house and keep her there."

Q11: What did Maya do while waiting for her husband to come home?

A) She slept

B) She read a book

C) She cooked dinner

D) She sat there, sobbing

Answer: D

Ex- The text states, "She sat there, sobbing, and waiting for her husband to come home."

Q12: How did the drive leading to the house look in the heat?

A) Like a frozen river

B) Like molten lead in a groove cut into the earth

C) Like a golden pathway

D) Like a dried-up stream

Answer: B

Ex- The text describes the drive as "shrivelling, melting and then shrivelling again, like molten lead in a groove cut into the earth."

Q13: Where was the small white corpse laying?

A) At one end of the verandah

B) At one end of the lawn

C) At one end of the driveway

D) At one end of the house

Answer: B

Ex- The text mentions, "the small white corpse laying at one end of the lawn, under a sheet, under the limes."

Q14: When did Maya's husband come home?

A) In the morning

B) At noon

C) In the early evening

D) Later in the evening

Answer: D

Ex- The text states, "Later in the evening, when the sun hung pendent from the topmost branches of the trees... her husband came home."

Q15: What did Maya's husband do as soon as he came home?

A) He had tea

B) He went to sleep

C) He telephoned the Public Works Department to send a scavenging truck

D) He scolded Maya for crying

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions, "He telephoned the Public Works Department, he had them send their scavenging truck to take the corpse away."

Q16: What did Maya's husband wipe his fingers on after the corpse was taken away?

A) A clean towel

B) A disposable tissue

C) A handkerchief much used and soiled during the day

D) His shirt

Answer: C

Ex- The text states, "he came to her, wiping his fingers on a handkerchief much used and soiled during the day."

Q17: What did Maya fear still hung in the evening air?

A) The stench of decaying flesh

B) The smell of the lime trees

C) The scent of the bougainvillaea flowers

D) The odor of the tea

Answer: A

Ex- The text mentions, "she would not lift her face from a cushion, for fear the stench of decaying flesh still hung in the bougainvillaea coloured evening air."

Q18: What color was the shade cast by the bougainvillaea flowers on Maya's skin?

A) Blue

B) Green

C) Purple

D) Red

Answer: C

Ex- The text states, "All day the purple flowers of the bougainvillaea had cast a flood of coloured shade on her skin."

Q19: What did Maya's husband say had been done with the corpse?

A) It was buried

B) It was thrown in the river

C) It was sent away to be cremated

D) It was left for the scavengers

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions, "'I sent it away to be cremated,' he said."

Q20: What was brought to the table beside Maya, as every other evening?

A) A coffee tray

B) A tea tray

C) A dinner tray

D) A dessert tray

Answer: B

Ex- The text states, "as every other evening, the tea-tray had been brought to the table beside her."

Q21: What items were on the tea tray?

A) A silver tea-pot, biscuit tin, and sliced lemon

B) A china tea-pot, cake stand, and sugar cubes

C) A ceramic tea-pot, cookie jar, and honey

D) A glass tea-pot, muffins, and jam

Answer: A

Ex- The text mentions, "the tea-tray had been brought to the table beside her, neatly decked with the grandmotherly silver tea-pot, the biscuit tin and the sliced lemon."

Q22: What happened when Maya tried to pour the tea?

A) Everything was served perfectly

B) The tea spilt into the sugar-pot, the tea-strainer toppled into a cup, the lemons slipped to the floor

C) She broke the tea-pot

D) She burnt her hand

Answer: B

Ex- The text states, "But it spilt into the sugar-pot, the tea-strainer toppled into a cup, the lemons slipped to the floor, and there was chaos."

Q23: Who came to announce a visitor?

A) The gardener

B) The sweeper

C) The servant

D) The neighbor

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions, "Just then the servant came into announce a visitor."

Q24: What was the profession of the visitor who came to see Maya's husband?

A) A doctor

B) An advocate

C) A priest

D) A businessman

Answer: B

Ex- The text states, "the servant came into announce a visitor — an advocate, he said, come to see the Sahib."

Q25: What did Maya do when her husband rose to meet the visitor, forgetting her woes?

A) She continued to serve tea

B) She went to the garden

C) She fled to the bedroom and flung herself onto the bed

D) She followed her husband to the study

Answer: C

Ex- The text mentions, "she cried, and fled to the bedroom to fling herself onto the bed."

Part II

### अभ्यास प्रश्न

1. Who is the author of the novel "Cry, The Peacock"?
   A. Salman Rushdie
   B. Anita Desai
   C. Arundhati Roy
   D. Vikram Seth
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text clearly states that "Cry, The Peacock" is the first novel by Anita Desai.

2. What is the name of the protagonist in the novel?
   A. Maya
   B. Gautama
   C. Rai Sahib
   D. Anita
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that the story is about Maya, the daughter of a rich lawyer in Lucknow, India.

3. What happened to Maya's mother?
   A. She died when Maya was young
   B. She left the family
   C. She was institutionalized
   D. She moved to the US
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya loses her mother to an early death.

4. Who advised Maya's father to protect her from reality?
   A. The family priest
   B. The family doctor
   C. The astrologer
   D. Gautama
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that the family doctor advised Maya's father to protect her from reality.

5. What does Maya's father do to protect her from reality?
   A. Sends her to a boarding school
   B. Tells her fairy tales and surrounds her with beautiful things
   C. Arranges her marriage with Gautama
   D. Sends her to the US with her brother
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya's father tells her fairy tales and surrounds her with beautiful things to protect her from reality.

6. What was the astrologer's prediction about Maya's marriage?
   A. She would have a happy marriage
   B. She would have many children
   C. Either she or her husband would die within four years of their marriage
   D. She would become a wealthy widow
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that the astrologer predicted that either Maya or her husband would die within four years of their marriage.

7. Who does Maya marry?
   A. Her childhood friend
   B. Her brother's friend
   C. Her father's friend
   D. A stranger
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya marries Gautama, her father's friend.

8. What is Gautama's profession?
   A. Doctor
   B. Businessman
   C. Lawyer
   D. Professor
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Gautama is another lawyer, like Maya's father.

9. What is the main reason for the growing distance between Maya and Gautama?
   A. Gautama's infidelity
   B. Maya's love for another man
   C. Gautama's involvement in his profession
   D. Maya's mental illness
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that the distance between Maya and Gautama grows because he is too involved in his profession to give her attention.

10. What does Gautama expect from Maya?
   A. To act rationally
   B. To have children
   C. To be a housewife
   D. To leave him alone
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Gautama expects his wife Maya to act rationally.

11. Why didn't Maya's father tell Gautama about her childhood neurosis?
   A. He forgot about it
   B. He didn't think it was important
   C. He feared it would scare Gautama off
   D. Gautama already knew about it
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya's father did not tell Gautama about her childhood neurosis, fearing that such a revelation would scare him off.

12. How long was Maya's marriage with Gautama?
   A. Two years
   B. Three years
   C. Four years
   D. Five years
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that after four years of unhappy marriage, Maya realizes that the astrologer's prediction is about to come true.

13. What does Maya fear as the fourth year of her marriage approaches?
   A. Gautama will leave her
   B. She will go insane
   C. She will die
   D. Gautama will die
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that as the fourth year of her marriage approaches, Maya fears that she will die, according to the astrologer's prediction.

14. What does Maya associate the peacock's cry with?
   A. Happiness
   B. Love
   C. Freedom
   D. Death
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya associates the peacock's cry, the wild shrieking for a lover, with a cry for death.

15. What does Maya do to relieve her agony?
   A. She takes medication
   B. She goes on a vacation
   C. She shifts the predicted death from herself to her husband
   D. She files for divorce
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya relieves her agony by shifting the predicted death from herself to her husband.

16. Why does Maya plan to murder Gautama?
   A. Because she hates him
   B. Because she loves life
   C. Because she wants to inherit his wealth
   D. Because she wants to marry someone else
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya plans to murder Gautama not because she hates him but because she loves life.

17. What happens on the day Maya kills Gautama?
   A. There is a fierce dust storm
   B. It is their wedding anniversary
   C. Gautama loses his job
   D. Maya's father dies
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Gautama comes home late on the day of a fierce dust storm.

18. Where does Maya lead Gautama before killing him?
   A. To the garden
   B. To the basement
   C. To the terrace on the roof
   D. To the bedroom
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya leads Gautama to the terrace on the roof so they can enjoy some fresh air.

19. What frightens Maya's cat as they go up the stairs?
   A. The sound of thunder
   B. The sight of a ghost
   C. The statue of Shiva
   D. The text doesn't mention
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya's cat rushes by them in a fright as they go up the stairs, but doesn't specify the reason.

20. What does Maya see on the stairs before going to the terrace?
   A. A statue of Shiva in his manifestation of creation and destruction
   B. A painting of the peacock
   C. A photograph of her mother
   D. A broken mirror
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya sees a statue of Shiva in his manifestation of creation and destruction as she goes up the stairs.

21. What is Maya enraptured by on the terrace?
   A. The city lights
   B. The rising moon
   C. The stars
   D. The peacock's cry
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that on the terrace, Maya is enraptured by the 'pale hushed glow of the rising moon.'

22. How does Maya kill Gautama?
   A. She stabs him with a knife
   B. She pushes him off the terrace
   C. She poisons him
   D. She strangles him
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that when Gautama moves in front of Maya, blocking the moon from her view, she pushes him over the parapet and he falls to his death.

23. Who removes Maya from the scene after Gautama's death?
   A. The police
   B. The neighbors
   C. Her father
   D. Her brother
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya's father comes and removes the completely insane Maya from the scene after Gautama's death.

24. What happens to Maya after Gautama's death?
   A. She is arrested
   B. She is institutionalized
   C. She remarries
   D. She commits suicide
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that later, Maya commits suicide by jumping from another balcony.

25. What is the overriding theme of the novel?
   A. Revenge
   B. Love
   C. Ambition
   D. Betrayal
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that the overriding theme of this sorrowful novel is love, including parental love, maternal love, and conjugal love.

26. What does the author explore regarding love in childhood?
   A. Its irrelevance to adult life
   B. Its supreme importance to the development of a balanced individual
   C. Its potential for causing neurosis
   D. Its transient nature
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that the author explores the necessity of love in childhood, suggesting its supreme importance to the development of a balanced individual.

27. What is absent in Maya's life after she grows up and gets married?
   A. Paternal love
   B. Sibling love
   C. Maternal love
   D. Self-love
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that maternal love is also absent when Maya grows up and gets married, as she remains childless.

28. What is hinted at regarding Maya's relationship with her father?
   A. A Freudian father-fixation
   B. A distant relationship
   C. A purely platonic bond
   D. A business partnership
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that there is more than a hint of a Freudian father-fixation by Maya due to the excessive love from her father.

29. What is the more destructive factor in Maya's life?
   A. The lack of love from her husband
   B. The death of her pet dog
   C. The absence of her brother
   D. The loss of her mother
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that the more destructive factor in Maya's life is the lack of love from her husband, Gautama.

30. What drives Maya to her murderous madness?
   A. The loss of love in childhood
   B. The departure of her beloved brother
   C. The lack of love in marriage
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that the loss of love in childhood, the departure of a beloved brother, the lack of love in marriage, and the absence of a child as an object of a mother's love are what drive Maya to her murderous madness.

31. What is Maya's state of mind at the beginning of the story?
   A. Joyful
   B. Neurotic
   C. Apathetic
   D. Calm
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that even in the beginning of the story, Maya is a neurotic young woman, haunted by loss and death.

32. What tragedy befalls Maya on the first page of the novel?
   A. Her father dies
   B. Her husband dies
   C. Her brother dies
   D. Her pet dog dies
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that on the first page of the novel, Maya's pet dog dies.

33. Who predicted that either Maya or her husband would die within four years of their marriage?
   A. A fortune teller
   B. An astrologer
   C. A priest
   D. A doctor
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya is terrified by the prediction of an astrologer that either she or her husband would die within four years of their marriage.

34. What does Maya want from her husband that he is incapable of showing?
   A. Wealth
   B. Status
   C. Warmth and love
   D. Intelligence
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya wants warmth and love from her husband, which she got from her father, but which her husband is incapable of showing.

35. Who does Maya believe loves her the most?
   A. Her mother
   B. Her brother
   C. Her husband
   D. Her father
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text quotes Maya lamenting to herself that '[n]o one, no one else, loves me as my father does.'

36. What kind of person is Maya described as?
   A. Extremely sensitive and sensuous
   B. Cold and calculating
   C. Outgoing and sociable
   D. Rational and logical
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text describes Maya as an extremely sensitive and sensuous person, who wants 'contact that goes deeper than the flesh.'

37. What aspects of nature affect Maya deeply?
   A. Colors and the call of birds
   B. Storms and earthquakes
   C. Oceans and mountains
   D. Deserts and cacti
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya is affected deeply by colors and by nature, especially the call of birds, such as the peacock.

38. What does Maya's sensitivity lead to?
   A. Artistic talent
   B. Psychosis and total madness
   C. Spiritual enlightenment
   D. Scientific curiosity
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya's sensitivity leads her to the point of psychosis, and later total madness.

39. What does Maya become in the end, haunted by the prediction and all alone?
   A. A nun
   B. A recluse
   C. A murderess
   D. A poet
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that in the end, haunted by the prediction and all alone, Maya becomes a murderess.

40. As a young girl, where does Maya often play with her widowed father?
   A. In the garden
   B. In the library
   C. In the kitchen
   D. In the attic
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that as a young girl, Maya plays in the garden, often with her widowed father.

41. What does Maya's imagination allow her to do?
   A. Turn objects into playthings and ordinary sadness into nightmares
   B. Communicate with spirits
   C. Predict the future
   D. Fly like a bird
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya has the imagination to turn objects into playthings and ordinary sadness into nightmares.

42. After her marriage, where does Maya spend most of her time?
   A. At her husband's office
   B. At her friend's house
   C. At home, inside or in the garden
   D. Traveling the world
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that after her marriage, Maya broods most of the time at home, inside or in the garden.

43. What is Gautama's profession?
   A. Doctor
   B. Teacher
   C. Lawyer
   D. Businessman
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text describes Gautama as a lawyer who marries Maya.

44. How does Gautama respond to Maya's challenge about only feeling 'bored' at a party?
   A. He apologizes for his lack of emotion
   B. He defends himself by saying that boredom was just a fact
   C. He accuses her of being too emotional
   D. He ignores her comment
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that when Maya challenges Gautama for only feeling 'bored' at a party, he defends himself by saying that the boredom was just a fact.

45. What is Gautama's attitude towards facts?
   A. Facts are made to be accepted and studied, not to be wept over
   B. Facts are irrelevant in the face of emotions
   C. Facts are meant to be challenged and questioned
   D. Facts are less important than intuition
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gautama says, 'Facts are made to be accepted and to be studied. Not to be wept over.'

46. How does Gautama show kindness to Maya?
   A. By giving her expensive gifts
   B. By taking care of her when she is ill
   C. By spending all his free time with her
   D. By encouraging her to pursue her hobbies
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Gautama takes care of Maya when she is ill, has headaches, or lies in bed with some kind of 'female ailment.'

47. Despite his kindness, how does Gautama treat Maya?
   A. He is supportive and understanding
   B. He is indifferent and uncaring
   C. He is censorious and constantly tells her to 'sit up' or 'change your sari'
   D. He is abusive and violent
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that despite his superficial kindness, Gautama is censorious and constantly tells Maya to 'sit up' or 'change your sari.'

48. Where does Gautama spend long hours working?
   A. In his office in the city and at home
   B. In the garden
   C. At the library
   D. At his friend's house
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Gautama spends long hours in his office, not only the one in the city but also the one in his home.

49. What does Gautama read besides case law?
   A. Poetry
   B. Novels
   C. Religious texts, like the Bhagavad Gita
   D. Scientific journals
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Gautama also reads religious texts, like the Bhagavad Gita, in addition to case law.

50. How does Gautama respond when Maya suggests going on a holiday?
   A. He agrees enthusiastically
   B. He says it's unlikely because he's too busy with work
   C. He says they can't afford it
   D. He ignores her suggestion
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- When Maya suggests going on a holiday, Gautama says, 'Unlikely,' snapping open his cigarette case with a metallic click that matched his voice.

51. What do Gautama's family members talk about in order to relax?
   A. Love and affection
   B. Politics and government
   C. Art and literature
   D. Their personal lives
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that in order to relax, Gautama's family members sometimes play cards, but they play so intensely that they find they have to release the mounting pressure by conversing and begin to talk again of politics and government.

52. How does Gautama handle the situation when Maya's pet dog dies?
   A. He comforts Maya and grieves with her
   B. He scolds Maya for being too attached to the dog
   C. He does all that has to be done, quickly and quietly like a surgeon's knife at work
   D. He ignores the situation and leaves Maya to deal with it alone
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that when Maya's pet dog dies, Gautama does all that has to be done, quickly and quietly like a surgeon's knife at work.

53. What is Rai Sahib's profession?
   A. Doctor
   B. Businessman
   C. Retired lawyer
   D. Politician
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Rai Sahib is a retired lawyer.

54. Where was Rai Sahib educated?
   A. India
   B. England
   C. America
   D. France
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Rai Sahib was educated in England.

55. What did Rai Sahib do during the fight against the British for Independence?
   A. He fought on the side of the British
   B. He remained neutral
   C. He spent time in prison
   D. He fled the country
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Rai Sahib spent time in prison during the fight against the British for Independence.

56. How is Rai Sahib portrayed in the novel?
   A. As a well-rounded character with a lot of depth
   B. As 'the father', without much depth of character
   C. As a minor character with little significance to the plot
   D. As the main antagonist
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Rai Sahib is portrayed as 'the father', without much depth of character, because that is how he is perceived by Maya, whose disturbed psychology is at the heart of this searing drama.

57. What does Rai Sahib love as a result of his legal training?
   A. Chaos and unpredictability
   B. Spontaneity and flexibility
   C. Order, patterns, habits, and repetitions
   D. Change and novelty
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that given his legal training, it is not surprising that Rai Sahib is a very disciplined man, who loves order, patterns, habits, and repetitions.

58. How does Rai Sahib's affection for Maya come across?
   A. As the sweet attention of an older father
   B. As cold and distant
   C. As manipulative and controlling
   D. As inappropriate and disturbing
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Rai Sahib's affection can come across as the sweet attention of an older father.

59. What does Rai Sahib do every day in his retirement?
   A. He travels the world
   B. He writes his memoirs
   C. He maintains and inspects his garden
   D. He volunteers at a local charity
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Rai Sahib spends a lot of time maintaining his garden, which becomes his arena for discipline in his retirement. He inspects it every day.

60. How does Rai Sahib react when a woman with loose, straggling hair arrives sobbing in his garden?
   A. He comforts her and offers her shelter
   B. He ignores her and continues with his breakfast
   C. He is appalled at how human beings can be 'a magnet for disorder and failure'
   D. He asks her to leave immediately
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- After the sobbing woman leaves, Rai Sahib explains to Maya that he is appalled at how human beings can be 'a magnet for disorder and failure.' Part I

61. What lay rotting in the sun all day?
   A. A dead branch
   B. A dead animal
   C. A dead human body
   D. A dead bird
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "All day the body lay rotting in the sun."

62. Why couldn't the body be moved onto the verandah?
   A. It was too heavy to move
   B. The reek of dead flesh was overpowering in the April heat
   C. The verandah was too small
   D. The family wanted it to remain in the sun
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "It could not be moved onto the verandah for in that April heat, the reek of dead flesh was overpowering."

63. Where was the string bed with the body moved to?
   A. Under the mango trees
   B. Under the banyan tree
   C. Under the lime trees
   D. Under the neem trees
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "So she moved the little string bed on which it lay under the lime trees."

64. What did Maya do when she saw the corpse's eyes open and staring?
   A. She fainted
   B. She called for help
   C. She screamed and rushed to the garden tap to wash the vision from her eyes
   D. She sat down and cried
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "saw its eyes open and staring still, screamed and rushed to the garden tap to wash the vision from her eyes."

65. Who drew the corpse's eyelids down?
   A. Maya
   B. Maya's husband
   C. The gardener
   D. The sweeper
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "The gardener came and drew its eyelids down with two horny thumbs."

66. Who was supposed to bury the corpse?
   A. The gardener
   B. Maya
   C. Maya's husband
   D. The sweeper
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "Often in the course of that day he said to her patiently, 'The sweeper will do it.'"

67. Where had the sweeper gone?
   A. To the market
   B. To visit his brother
   C. To the temple
   D. To another village
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "He has gone to visit his brother but when he returns he will do it."

68. What birds sat in a circle around the corpse?
   A. Vultures
   B. Hawks
   C. Crows
   D. Eagles
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "Crows sat in a circle around the corpse."

69. What insects began to hum amidst the limes?
   A. Mosquitoes
   B. Flies
   C. Bees
   D. Butterflies
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "Flies began to hum amidst the limes."

70. Who sent his wife to take Maya into the house?
   A. The sweeper
   B. The gardener
   C. Maya's husband
   D. The visitor
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "The gardener sent his wife to take her into the house and keep her there."

71. What did Maya do while waiting for her husband to come home?
   A. She slept
   B. She read a book
   C. She cooked dinner
   D. She sat there, sobbing
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "She sat there, sobbing, and waiting for her husband to come home."

72. How did the drive leading to the house look in the heat?
   A. Like a frozen river
   B. Like molten lead in a groove cut into the earth
   C. Like a golden pathway
   D. Like a dried-up stream
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text describes the drive as "shrivelling, melting and then shrivelling again, like molten lead in a groove cut into the earth."

73. Where was the small white corpse laying?
   A. At one end of the verandah
   B. At one end of the lawn
   C. At one end of the driveway
   D. At one end of the house
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "the small white corpse laying at one end of the lawn, under a sheet, under the limes."

74. When did Maya's husband come home?
   A. In the morning
   B. At noon
   C. In the early evening
   D. Later in the evening
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "Later in the evening, when the sun hung pendent from the topmost branches of the trees... her husband came home."

75. What did Maya's husband do as soon as he came home?
   A. He had tea
   B. He went to sleep
   C. He telephoned the Public Works Department to send a scavenging truck
   D. He scolded Maya for crying
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "He telephoned the Public Works Department, he had them send their scavenging truck to take the corpse away."

76. What did Maya's husband wipe his fingers on after the corpse was taken away?
   A. A clean towel
   B. A disposable tissue
   C. A handkerchief much used and soiled during the day
   D. His shirt
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "he came to her, wiping his fingers on a handkerchief much used and soiled during the day."

77. What did Maya fear still hung in the evening air?
   A. The stench of decaying flesh
   B. The smell of the lime trees
   C. The scent of the bougainvillaea flowers
   D. The odor of the tea
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "she would not lift her face from a cushion, for fear the stench of decaying flesh still hung in the bougainvillaea coloured evening air."

78. What color was the shade cast by the bougainvillaea flowers on Maya's skin?
   A. Blue
   B. Green
   C. Purple
   D. Red
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "All day the purple flowers of the bougainvillaea had cast a flood of coloured shade on her skin."

79. What did Maya's husband say had been done with the corpse?
   A. It was buried
   B. It was thrown in the river
   C. It was sent away to be cremated
   D. It was left for the scavengers
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "'I sent it away to be cremated,' he said."

80. What was brought to the table beside Maya, as every other evening?
   A. A coffee tray
   B. A tea tray
   C. A dinner tray
   D. A dessert tray
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "as every other evening, the tea-tray had been brought to the table beside her."

81. What items were on the tea tray?
   A. A silver tea-pot, biscuit tin, and sliced lemon
   B. A china tea-pot, cake stand, and sugar cubes
   C. A ceramic tea-pot, cookie jar, and honey
   D. A glass tea-pot, muffins, and jam
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "the tea-tray had been brought to the table beside her, neatly decked with the grandmotherly silver tea-pot, the biscuit tin and the sliced lemon."

82. What happened when Maya tried to pour the tea?
   A. Everything was served perfectly
   B. The tea spilt into the sugar-pot, the tea-strainer toppled into a cup, the lemons slipped to the floor
   C. She broke the tea-pot
   D. She burnt her hand
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "But it spilt into the sugar-pot, the tea-strainer toppled into a cup, the lemons slipped to the floor, and there was chaos."

83. Who came to announce a visitor?
   A. The gardener
   B. The sweeper
   C. The servant
   D. The neighbor
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "Just then the servant came into announce a visitor."

84. What was the profession of the visitor who came to see Maya's husband?
   A. A doctor
   B. An advocate
   C. A priest
   D. A businessman
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states, "the servant came into announce a visitor — an advocate, he said, come to see the Sahib."

85. What did Maya do when her husband rose to meet the visitor, forgetting her woes?
   A. She continued to serve tea
   B. She went to the garden
   C. She fled to the bedroom and flung herself onto the bed
   D. She followed her husband to the study
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions, "she cried, and fled to the bedroom to fling herself onto the bed." Part II Chapter 1

## Main study material

Chapter 1

Q1: What prevented Maya from fully releasing her tears?

A) A sense of unease and hesitance

B) Exhaustion from crying

C) Lack of emotional connection to her pet

D) Fear of expressing her emotions

Answer: A

Explanation: Maya mentions a "certain unease, a hesitance in the air" that kept her tears from being fully released.

Q2: What did Maya believe she was mourning, in addition to her pet's death?

A) A forgotten memory

B) An unremembered sorrow

C) A future experience

D) All of the above

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya admits she was mourning "another sorrow, unremembered, perhaps as yet not even experienced."

Q3: How did Gautama attempt to comfort Maya?

A) By offering her an opal ring

B) By suggesting she needs a cup of tea

C) By telling her to go to sleep

D) By holding her close and wiping her tears

Answer: B

Explanation: When Maya was grieving, Gautama said, "You need a cup of tea," showing his lack of understanding of her misery.

Q4: What did Maya feel was the reason for her loneliness in the house?

A) Gautama's hardness

B) Gautama's coldness and emotional distance

C) Gautama's incessant talk of philosophy

D) Gautama's focus on his work

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya cried, "Yes, it is his hardness — no, no, not hardness, but the distance he coldly keeps from me. His coldness, his coldness..."

Q5: What did Maya do to exhaust herself after realizing her loneliness?

A) Crying

B) Pillow-beating and kicking

C) Running around the house

D) Screaming into a pillow

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya "gave myself up to a fit of furious pillow-beating, kicking, everything but crying" to exhaust herself.

Q6: What did Maya become more aware of when her wildest passions were over?

A) The rhythm of her pulse

B) The crepitations of the mind and the coldness of the heart

C) The evening breeze coming through the window

D) The distant thoughts streaking across her awareness

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya realized that when her greatest furies calmed, she "grew most keenly aware of the crepitations of the mind, the strange coldness of the heart."

Q7: What relationship is said to be no less worthy of reverence than that of a woman and her child?

A) A woman and her husband

B) A woman and her pet

C) A woman and her best friend

D) A woman and her sibling

Answer: B

Explanation: The passage states, "Childless women do develop fanatic attachments to their pets, they say. It is no less a relationship than that of a woman and her child, no less worthy of reverence, and agonized remembrance."

Q8: What did Maya promise to never forget?

A) Her pet's name

B) The fond names she had for her pet

C) The moments she shared with her pet

D) The love she had for her pet

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya promised, with parted lips and clasped hands, to never forget "the fond names I had had for my pet."

Q9: What did Maya turn to when abstract thinking was not successful for her?

A) Remembrance of a lullaby from her childhood

B) Thoughts of Gautama's tenderness

C) Memories of her pet

D) Imagining a future without her pet

Answer: A

Explanation: When abstract thinking was not successful, Maya "turned to remembrance of a lullaby that was crooned to me as a child a gentle, poignant lullaby that I murmured to yself."

Q10: How did Gautama's tenderness affect Maya when he came to her?

A) She was filled with anger

B) She was flooded with tenderness and gratitude

C) She felt indifferent towards him

D) She pushed him away

Answer: B

Explanation: When Gautama touched her hair and spoke to her softly, Maya "was flooded with tenderness and gratitude."

Q11: What did Gautama suggest they do after Maya stopped crying?

A) Stay in the bedroom

B) Go for a walk in the garden

C) Have dinner with the servants

D) Discuss philosophy

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama said, "Wipe your face, and we'll go out, Maya," suggesting they go outside in the garden.

Q12: How did the darkness in the garden affect Maya?

A) It shocked her like a strong and effective medicine

B) It made her feel agreeably like a child

C) It reminded her of her pet's burial

D) It made her feel more alone

Answer: A

Explanation: The passage states, "The balm of darkness met me with a little shock, like a strong and effective medicine on a wound still fresh."

Q13: What did Gautama do when Maya took his arm in the garden?

A) He held her close

B) He moved away, pretending to gesture to the gardener

C) He told her he detested when she held his arm

D) He led her back inside the house

Answer: B

Explanation: When Maya took Gautama's arm, "He tolerated my hold for a moment, in consideration of my bereavement, I expect, then moved away, pretending to gesture to the gardener."

Q14: What were the rituals of the evening comforting to Maya?

A) The preparations for the night

B) The gardener sprinkling the lawn

C) The servants taking the beds out

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: The passage states, "The rituals of the evening, the preparations for the night, all were vastly comforting to me."

Q15: What prevented Maya from fully crying and finding relief in her tears?

A) Gautama's presence

B) The realization that she was mourning more than her pet's death

C) The memory of her pet's soft, willing body

D) The hardness of Gautama's pillow

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya admits that "it was not my pet's death alone that I mourned today, but another sorrow, unremembered, perhaps as yet not even experienced, and filled me with this despair."

Q16: What did Maya try to focus on to trace the source of her disturbance?

A) The rhythm of her pulse

B) The evening breeze coming through the window

C) The distant and dark thoughts streaking across her awareness

D) The fond names she had for her pet

Answer: A

Explanation: Maya "listened to the throb of my pulse" and tried to think of it as a rhythm that would carry her back to the source of her disturbance.

Q17: What did Maya realize about the concept of "never" when promising to never forget her pet?

A) It was a meaningful promise

B) It was linked to the chain of time

C) It was an impossible promise to keep

D) It was the only way to truly remember her pet

Answer: C

Explanation: Maya became "immediately made aware of the moments trickling past" and realized "There was no such thing as never. Only now. And yesterday."

Q18: What did Gautama do when he found Maya lying in the dark?

A) He scolded her for not being outside

B) He touched her hair and smoothed it down carefully

C) He told her to stop crying and move on

D) He ignored her and went about his evening routine

Answer: B

Explanation: When Gautama found Maya lying in the dark, "he touched my hair, smoothing it down carefully as a nurse would."

Q19: What did Gautama take out to wipe Maya's face when she began crying again?

A) A tissue

B) A stained handkerchief

C) A cloth napkin

D) His sleeve

Answer: B

Explanation: When Maya began crying again, Gautama "took out the handkerchief again, more stained than ever."

Q20: What did Gautama firmly help Maya do after wiping her face?

A) Get out of bed

B) Change her clothes

C) Brush her hair

D) Eat something

Answer: A

Explanation: "Firmly he helped me out of bed, waited until I had found my slippers and smoothed my clothes, then led me out on the verandah, down the steps into the garden."

Q21: What did Maya know Gautama detested her doing?

A) Crying in front of him

B) Talking about her pet

C) Taking his arm

D) Going for walks in the garden

Answer: C

Explanation: When Maya took Gautama's arm in the garden, she "knew he detested me to do so."

Q22: What was the gardener doing when Gautama and Maya went outside?

A) Planting flowers

B) Trimming the hedges

C) Sprinkling the lawn

D) Sweeping the verandah

Answer: C

Explanation: When Gautama and Maya went outside, the gardener "was sprinkling the lawn so as to lay the dust before the beds were brought out."

Q23: What did Maya believe was the reason for Gautama's tolerance of her holding his arm?

A) He genuinely wanted to comfort her

B) He was considerate of her bereavement

C) He enjoyed her touch

D) He wanted to appear caring in front of the servants

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya believed Gautama "tolerated my hold for a moment, in consideration of my bereavement."

Q24: What did Gautama do after tolerating Maya's hold on his arm for a moment?

A) He held her closer

B) He gently removed her hand

C) He moved away, pretending to gesture to the gardener

D) He told her to let go

Answer: C

Explanation: After tolerating Maya's hold for a moment, Gautama "moved away, pretending to gesture to the gardener."

Q25: What did Maya find vastly comforting at the end of the passage?

A) Gautama's presence

B) The darkness of the garden

C) The rituals of the evening and preparations for the night

D) The memory of her pet

Answer: C

Explanation: The passage states, "The rituals of the evening, the preparations for the night, all were vastly comforting to me."

Q1: Where were the verandah chairs taken out for Maya and Gautama to sit?

A) On the porch

B) On the lawn

C) In the living room

D) In the bedroom

Answer: B

Explanation: The passage states, "The verandah chairs had been taken out on the lawn for us."

Q2: What did Maya and Gautama do each evening?

A) Have dinner together

B) Watch the sunset

C) Sit in comfortable cane chairs and have glasses of fresh lemonade

D) Go for a walk in the garden

Answer: C

Explanation: Maya and Gautama sat down each evening "to glasses of fresh lemonade and to an hour or so of matrimonial silences and conversation."

Q3: What type of plant entwined the pillars and climbed the walls of the verandah?

A) Jasmine

B) Bougainvillea

C) Rangoon creeper

D) Honeysuckle

Answer: C

Explanation: "Rangoon creeper entwined these pillars and climbed the walls, spread trembling tendrils towards the roof."

Q4: What color were the Rangoon creeper flowers in daylight?

A) Pink and red

B) White

C) Yellow and orange

D) Purple and blue

Answer: A

Explanation: The Rangoon creeper flowers "had been pink and red in daylight, and now were white and strongly scented."

Q5: What is said to attract snakes, according to the passage?

A) The sweet, intoxicating fragrance of Rangoon creeper

B) The sweet, intoxicating fragrance of Queen of the Night

C) The sound of crickets chirping

D) The presence of small rodents

Answer: B

Explanation: The passage states, "It is Queen of the Night that attracts snakes."

Q6: What season had just ended, and which season was yet to arrive?

A) Autumn had ended, winter was yet to arrive

B) Winter had ended, spring was yet to arrive

C) Winter had ended, summer was yet to arrive

D) Spring had ended, summer was yet to arrive

Answer: C

Explanation: "Winter was over, summer had not yet arrived."

Q7: What did Maya feel was irking her at the back of her mind?

A) A grain of sand

B) A sense of unease

C) A physical shadow

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: Maya felt "that undefinable unease at the back of my mind, the grain of sand that irked, itched, and remained meaningless," and "its presence was very real, and a truly physical shadow."

Q8: What sensation overcame Maya, causing her to leap from her chair in terror?

A) A sensation of snakes coiling and uncoiling their moist lengths about her

B) A sensation of evil descending from an overhanging branch

C) A sensation of an insane death, unprepared for, heralded by deafening drum-beats

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Explanation: Maya was "overcome by a sensation of snakes coiling and uncoiling their moist lengths about me, of evil descending from an overhanging branch, of an insane death, unprepared for, heralded by deafening drum-beats."

Q9: What did Maya realize was causing the pounding sensation against her ear-drums?

A) The sound of crickets

B) The sound of her own blood

C) The sound of drums in the distance

D) The sound of the servants running towards her

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya realized the pounding sensation was "only been the blood pounding against my ear-drums."

Q10: What flew out of the tree when someone switched on a table-lamp?

A) A bat

B) A moth

C) An owl

D) A bird

Answer: C

Explanation: When someone switched on a table-lamp, "an owl flew out of the tree, hooting with merriment."

Q11: What did Maya insist on doing after the panic subsided?

A) Sitting back down in the chair

B) Going inside the house

C) Walking up and down with Gautama

D) Continuing the conversation with the servants

Answer: C

Explanation: Maya said, "But I wouldn't sit here. Let us walk, Gautama, let us walk up and down."

Q12: How did Gautama describe his voice when trying to reassure Maya?

A) Reassuring, logical, and calm

B) Soothing, gentle, and kind

C) Firm, authoritative, and commanding

D) Excited, animated, and lively

Answer: A

Explanation: Gautama's voice was described as "reassuring, logical, calm."

Q13: What did Gautama suggest as a "straw to cling to" regarding the pet's death?

A) The pet did not really suffer long

B) The pet's death was peaceful

C) The pet was old and had lived a full life

D) The pet was now in a better place

Answer: A

Explanation: Gautama said, "If you would like a straw to cling to, then here is one — he did not suffer long."

Q14: What did Gautama believe the vet would have done if they had arrived?

A) Saved the pet's life

B) Eased the pet's suffering

C) Prolonged the pet's pain

D) Performed a miracle

Answer: C

Explanation: Gautama said, "The vet would only have prolonged the pain, in a case like this."

Q15: What did Maya know about the pet's condition, even though she wished the vet could have helped?

A) The pet's condition was treatable

B) The pet's condition was hopeless

C) The pet's condition was improving

D) The pet's condition was uncertain

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya "whimpered, knowing it had been hopeless" when she said, "He might have helped."

Q16: What sensation did Gautama's voice create in Maya's mind?

A) A sensation of walking through a bright and sunny day

B) A sensation of walking through a dark and wet night with somebody beside her who carried a lantern, a staff, and a blanket

C) A sensation of floating on a calm and peaceful lake

D) A sensation of being lost in a dense and confusing forest

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama's voice created "a sensation, a much loved, long courted sensation, of walking through a dark and wet night with somebody beside me who carried a lantern, a staff and a blanket" in Maya's mind.

Q17: What did Maya do instinctively when Gautama's voice created this sensation?

A) Moved closer to him

B) Moved away from him

C) Stopped walking

D) Started running

Answer: A

Explanation: When Gautama's voice created the sensation, Maya "moved closer to him, instinctively."

Q18: What did Gautama stop to do while talking to Maya?

A) Tie his shoelace

B) Light a cigarette

C) Adjust his shirt

D) Check his watch

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama stopped "to light a cigarette" while talking to Maya.

Q19: What was Gautama sorry about regarding Maya's experience that day?

A) That Maya had to witness the pet's death

B) That Maya had been alone with the pet's body all day, with no one to put it away

C) That Maya had to make the decision to euthanize the pet

D) That Maya had to break the news of the pet's death to the family

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama said, "I am only sorry about your having been alone with the body all day, with no one to put it away."

Q20: What did Gautama assume had happened to the pet's body by the time he and Maya were talking?

A) It had been buried

B) It had been cremated

C) It had been taken away by the vet

D) It was still in the house

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama said, "But by now it must have been cremated."

Q21: What time of year was it when the events in the passage took place?

A) January

B) April

C) July

D) October

Answer: B

Explanation: The passage mentions "the dry, scented April dark" and "April stars," indicating the events took place in April.

Q22: What were the servants doing while Maya and Gautama were walking and talking?

A) Sprinkling the lawn and smoothing sheets

B) Preparing breakfast

C) Cleaning the house

D) Tending to the garden

Answer: A

Explanation: The passage states, "the servants went back to sprinkling the lawn, to smoothing sheets, to preparing dinner."

Q23: What did the Rangoon creeper flowers resemble when hanging in long bunches?

A) Red grapes

B) White grapes

C) Cherries

D) Plums

Answer: B

Explanation: The Rangoon creeper flowers "hung in long bunches, like those of white grapes."

Q24: What movement did the Rangoon creeper flowers make in the breeze?

A) A slow, mysterious movement as of nocturnal snakes

B) A fast, erratic movement as of startled birds

C) A gentle, swaying movement as of ocean waves

D) A circular, spinning movement as of falling leaves

Answer: A

Explanation: The Rangoon creeper flowers made "a slow, mysterious movement as of nocturnal snakes" when rising and descending upon the uneven breeze.

Q25: What did Maya compare the giant shadows cast by trees to, in terms of their effect on her?

A) A soothing blanket of darkness

B) A playful game of hide-and-seek

C) A horrifying swiftness that reached her toes and lapped her feet

D) A comforting embrace of nature

Answer: C

Explanation: The giant shadows cast by trees "spilt across the leaves and grasses towards me, with horrifying swiftness, till, like the crowding blades of grass, it reached my toes, lapped my feet, tickling and worrying."

Q1: How did Maya react when Gautama told her about the pet's body being cremated?

A) She drew closer to him

B) She drew away from him with a jerk

C) She remained indifferent

D) She started crying uncontrollably

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya "drew away from him with a jerk" and said, "No, stop. Why do you tell me this?"

Q2: How did Gautama view the ceremonies and trimmings associated with burying the dead?

A) As meaningful and important

B) As the ultimate absurdity and meaningless

C) As a necessary evil

D) As a way to honor the deceased

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama considered the ceremonies and trimmings associated with burying the dead "the ultimate absurdity, appropriate only in that it brings a meaningless life to a similarly meaningless end."

Q3: According to Gautama, what do people who have no greater values apply themselves to with passion?

A) Lesser values that are not values at all

B) Greater values that have a standard and criterion

C) A mix of greater and lesser values

D) No values at all

Answer: A

Explanation: Gautama said, "Having no greater values, they apply themselves with passion to the lesser ones — which are not values at all, of course, for values must have a standard, a criterion."

Q4: How did Maya listen to Gautama's philosophical musings?

A) With full attention and agreement

B) With complete disinterest and boredom

C) Half-attentively and half-pensively, with her thoughts straying

D) With anger and frustration

Answer: C

Explanation: Maya listened to Gautama "half-attentively, half-pensively, my thoughts straying from the straight, cobbled path of his march into side-lanes and by-lanes of my own choosing and my own making."

Q5: What did Maya sometimes offer to Gautama during their conversations?

A) A handful of rushes or an oddly shaped pebble

B) A cup of tea or a snack

C) A book or a newspaper article

D) A joke or a funny story

Answer: A

Explanation: Maya would sometimes return to Gautama "to see if he were still plodding along as he had been when I deserted him, coming to offer him a handful of rushes I had collected, or an oddly shaped pebble."

Q6: According to Gautama, whose lives are of importance and matter?

A) The majority of dim-wits and numbskulls

B) The few who lead the dim-wits and numbskulls, capable of logic and analysis

C) Everyone, regardless of their intelligence or status

D) No one's life matters or is of importance

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama believed that "It is only the few who lead these animals, the ones who are capable of logic and analysis, who matter. Their lives are of importance."

Q7: What did Gautama find confounding about intelligent, reasonable men?

A) Their dedication to the solidity of thought and facts

B) Their discussion of the beauty of burial vs. cremation with the same avidity as the "sheep"

C) Their lack of interest in philosophical matters

D) Their inability to lead the dim-wits and numbskulls

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama found it confounding that "intelligent, reasonable men — to discuss, with the same avidity the sheep have, the beauty of burial as opposed to the beauty of cremation, argue about it as though it were a fine point in theology."

Q8: What did Maya believe about burial and cremation, in contrast to Gautama's views?

A) They are facts that matter

B) They are meaningless and trivial

C) They are only important for the wealthy and powerful

D) They should be abolished altogether

Answer: A

Explanation: When Gautama dismissed burial and cremation as trivial, Maya argued, "Burial and cremation are facts, Gautama."

Q9: What did Maya say about the importance of pets in her life?

A) Pets are meaningless and do not matter

B) Pets are only important for children

C) Pets might not mean anything to Gautama, but they mean the world to her

D) Pets are a waste of time and resources

Answer: C

Explanation: Maya cried, "Oh, Gautama, pets might not mean anything to you, and yet they mean the world to me."

Q10: What did Maya believe about the desire for a splendid death and being remembered?

A) It is a foolish and vain desire

B) It is a desire held by those who do not value living

C) It is a desire that should be discouraged

D) It is a desire held by those who want to be remembered for ages by everybody

Answer: D

Explanation: Maya said, "Living mightn't mean much to some people, but when they die, they want it to happen splendidly, something that will be remembered, for ages, by everybody."

Q11: How did Gautama react when Maya mentioned the desire for immortality?

A) He was annoyed and dismissive

B) He was delighted and approving

C) He was confused and puzzled

D) He was indifferent and uninterested

Answer: B

Explanation: When Maya mentioned the desire for something that will be remembered, Gautama cried, "Ah!" and his white face sprung out "as though to pounce-upon something he had recognized, and approved."

Q12: According to Gautama, what is the only thing that continues to live?

A) Wealth and power

B) Tradition

C) Intelligence and logic

D) Love and compassion

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama said, "the only thing that continues to live is tradition."

Q13: What does Gautama believe about traditions that have endured?

A) They must be worthy of endurance

B) They are all outdated and should be discarded

C) They are only important for the older generation

D) They have no value or meaning

Answer: A

Explanation: Gautama said, "Having endured, they must be worthy of endurance, claim these simple souls, and thus they find something they can dedicate their small silly minds to with impunity."

Q14: What does Maya insist is the reason why traditions matter to people?

A) Because they are ancient and antediluvian

B) Because they are fashionable and impressive

C) Because they matter to them personally

D) Because they are easy to follow without thinking

Answer: C

Explanation: Maya insisted, "It is also because it matters to them."

Q15: What does Gautama believe the Gita preaches or recommends?

A) Involvement in tradition

B) Detachment on every count

C) Worship of plaster gods

D) Empty rites and rituals

Answer: B

Explanation: Gautama said, "The Gita does not preach involvement in tradition. It preaches — recommends, rather — detachment on every count."

Q16: What does Gautama believe would happen if people truly understood the Gita?

A) They would become more involved in traditions

B) They would worship plaster gods more fervently

C) They would be capable of disappearing without the paraphernalia of vulgarity

D) They would become more attached to empty rites and rituals

Answer: C

Explanation: Gautama said, "If only those who read it so piously, in front of their plaster gods, every morning and every night, would also understand it, then they would be capable of disappearing without drawing down upon their heads this paraphernalia of vulgarity."

Q17: How did Maya feel about Gautama's use of the word "disappearance"?

A) She found it comforting and reassuring

B) She found it cruel and painful

C) She found it amusing and funny

D) She found it inspiring and motivating

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya felt "a pang as the solidity of real existence gave forth one little gasp and collapsed, like a worm-eaten fruit, at my feet" when Gautama spoke of disappearance, calling it "a cruel word, cruelly spoken."

Q18: What memories of Toto did Maya think of in response to Gautama's words?

A) Toto's short stump of a white tail and the foolish little wisp at its end

B) Toto's laziness and lack of energy

C) Toto's aggression and bad behavior

D) Toto's indifference to Maya's presence

Answer: A

Explanation: In response to Gautama's words, Maya "thought of Toto's short stump of a white tail again, of the foolish little wisp at its end."

Q19: How did Toto react when Maya returned from a morning in town?

A) He ignored her and continued sleeping

B) He barked wildly and thrilledly, flinging himself upon her

C) He growled and showed aggression towards her

D) He hid from her and avoided contact

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya remembered "Toto's wild, thrilled bark as he saw me return from a morning in town, the impact of his body as he flung himself upon me."

Q20: What did Maya believe about the endurance of her memories of Toto compared to Gautama's "facts"?

A) Her memories were less enduring than Gautama's facts

B) Her memories were at least as enduring as Gautama's facts

C) Her memories had no endurance at all

D) Her memories were completely unrelated to Gautama's facts

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya believed that her memories of Toto "did not disappear" and "were at least as enduring as the 'facts' Gautama was continually extolling."

Q21: Why did Maya not ask Gautama aloud about the endurance of her memories?

A) She was afraid of his reaction

B) She thought her private thoughts were sufficient

C) She did not care about his opinion

D) She agreed with his perspective

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya did not ask Gautama aloud about the endurance of her memories because "These were private thoughts and having thought them was sufficient."

Q22: What did Maya believe she had to learn to be grateful for in her relationship with Gautama?

A) His intelligence and logical thinking

B) His presence alone, not the meanderings of his brain

C) His involvement in traditions and rituals

D) His emotional support and understanding

Answer: B

Explanation: Maya realized that "she had to learn to be grateful for his presence alone, not be disturbed by the meanderings of his brain."

Q23: How did Gautama's brain work when he was with Maya, according to the passage?

A) Like a clock spring winding itself tightly

B) Like a clock spring unwinding itself into relaxation

C) Like a well-oiled machine, efficiently and effectively

D) Like a rusty gear, struggling to function properly

Answer: B

Explanation: The passage states that Gautama's brain, "when with me, worked like a clock spring unwinding itself into relaxation."

Q24: What did Maya compare the collapse of the solidity of real existence to, based on Gautama's words?

A) A worm-eaten fruit

B) A deflated balloon

C) A shattered glass

D) A melted ice cube

Answer: A

Explanation: Maya felt that "the solidity of real existence gave forth one little gasp and collapsed, like a worm-eaten fruit, at my feet" in response to Gautama's words.

Q25: How did Maya respond to Gautama's philosophical musings and arguments?

A) She agreed with him completely and followed his logic

B) She disagreed with him vehemently and argued against his points

C) She listened half-attentively, with her thoughts straying, and occasionally interrupted him

D) She ignored him completely and changed the subject

Answer: C

Explanation: Throughout the passage, Maya listens to Gautama "half-attentively, half-pensively," with her thoughts straying, and occasionally interrupts him with her own perspectives and arguments.

Q1: What flowers does Maya notice in the garden?

A) Roses

B) Petunias

C) Daffodils

D) Tulips

Answer: B

Ex- Maya specifically mentions noticing petunias, describing them as "sentimental, irresolute flowers."

Q2: How does Maya describe the scent of the petunias?

A) Crisp and refreshing

B) Poignant, half-sweet, and half-sad

C) Strong and vivid

D) Sour and stringent

Answer: B

Ex- Maya describes the petunias' fragrance as "a poignant, half-sweet, half-sad fragrance that matched my mood to perfection."

Q3: What does Gautama compare the scent of the lemon blossoms to?

A) Ground lemon peel

B) Crushed lemon leaf

C) Mother-of-pearl

D) Moon shells

Answer: A

Ex- When Maya tries to explain the scent of the lemon blossoms, she describes it as "refreshing as that of ground lemon peel."

Q4: How long have Maya and Gautama been married?

A) One year

B) Two years

C) Three years

D) Four years

Answer: D

Ex- The text states that Maya and Gautama "had been married four years now."

Q5: What does Maya believe Gautama's visitor wanted to discuss?

A) A case of passion and revenge

B) A murder case

C) Money or property

D) Exciting things

Answer: C

Ex- Maya assumes Gautama's visitor wanted to discuss "some musty old case, about money, or property, or something dreary like that."

Q6: According to Gautama, what does love ultimately become for a man?

A) Worrying about bills

B) Dealing with bills

C) Rearing children

D) Paying for children's upbringing

Answer: B

Ex- Gautama states that love "ultimately becomes, you will find, for the man a matter or dealing with the bills that come in."

Q7: What does Gautama say about the relationship between reality and idealism?

A) They are distinct and separate

B) They are one and the same thing

C) Reality is more important than idealism

D) Idealism is more important than reality

Answer: B

Ex- Gautama explains that "reality and idealism are one and the same thing" and that life is about reconciling them.

Q8: What does Maya compare her thoughts of anguish to?

A) Flowers in a garden

B) Stars in the night sky

C) Birds waking from dreams

D) Petals on a flower

Answer: C

Ex- Maya's thoughts of anguish are described as rising "like birds that awake from dreams and rise out of their trees amidst great commotion."

Q9: What does Maya focus on to draw away from her pain?

A) The night sky

B) The stone urn

C) The white petunias

D) Gautama's presence

Answer: C

Ex- Maya bends over the white petunias, "inhaling that mist of sad, maidenly scent," to draw away from her pain.

Q10: What does Gautama say about the night sky?

A) It's a clear night

B) The stars are beautiful

C) The darkness is overwhelming

D) It reminds him of loneliness

Answer: A

Ex- Gautama looks up at the night sky and simply remarks, "What a clear night."

Q11: What does Maya associate with the spaces of darkness between the stars?

A) Beauty

B) Hope

C) Death

D) Rebirth

Answer: C

Ex- Maya thinks that "Death lurked in those spaces, the darkness spoke of distance, separation, loneliness."

Q12: What does Maya imagine the journey of the dead to be like?

A) A short trip to a new life

B) A brave traversing of mute darkness

C) A joyful experience

D) A journey filled with light

Answer: B

Ex- Maya thinks of "the long journey of the dead from one birth into another, the brave traversing of mute darkness."

Q13: Who is Toto?

A) Maya's husband

B) Maya's friend

C) Maya's pet

D) Gautama's visitor

Answer: C

Ex- Toto is mentioned as a small white corpse, implying he was Maya's pet who died.

Q14: How does Maya describe Toto's barks before his death?

A) Loud and energetic

B) Soft and comforting

C) Futile and dying away into silence

D) Happy and playful

Answer: C

Ex- Maya remembers "Small white Toto, whining a little, out of fear, his futile barks dying away into a hopeless silence."

Q15: What does Maya confess to Gautama about Toto?

A) She never liked Toto

B) She will miss Toto terribly

C) She blamed Gautama for Toto's death

D) She doesn't want another pet

Answer: B

Ex- Maya cries out to Gautama, "I shall miss him so terribly," referring to Toto.

Q16: How does Gautama respond to Maya's confession about missing Toto?

A) He ignores her

B) He scolds her for being emotional

C) He wipes away her tear

D) He walks away

Answer: C

Ex- Gautama raises his hand to wipe away Maya's tear as she confesses she will miss Toto terribly.

Q17: What is Gautama's hand compared to as he wipes away Maya's tear?

A) A soft petal

B) A cool breeze

C) The bark of an old and shady tree

D) A gentle wave

Answer: C

Ex- Gautama's hand is described as being "as cool and dry as the bark of an old and shady tree" as he wipes Maya's tear.

Q18: In what language does Gautama quote a couplet?

A) Hindi

B) English

C) Urdu

D) Sanskrit

Answer: C

Ex- After wiping Maya's tear, Gautama "quoted, softly, an Urdu couplet."

Q19: What does Maya find shocking about Gautama's speech?

A) His lack of emotion

B) His poetic language

C) The reduction of ideals to mundane superfluities

D) His agreement with her viewpoint

Answer: C

Ex- Maya finds it "rather shocking to reduce one's ideals to mundane superfluities," as Gautama does in his speech.

Q20: What does Maya believe death reveals ideals to be?

A) Meaningless

B) The same as the superfluities one has known and spurned

C) More important than reality

D) Separate from reality

Answer: B

Ex- Maya realizes that it is "equally shocking to realize that these superfluities are the ideals themselves."

Q21: What season has recently ended in the story?

A) Spring

B) Summer

C) Autumn

D) Winter

Answer: D

Ex- The text mentions "a poignant, half-sweet, half-sad fragrance that matched my mood to perfection, and I started to it with the embrace of recognition, taking in deep breaths of this gentle, fading odour that was so laden with wistful remembrance of the winter."

Q22: How does Maya describe the shape of the lemon blossom petals?

A) Round and soft

B) Triangular and pointed

C) Curving and scimitar-like

D) Flat and oval

Answer: C

Ex- Maya describes the lemon blossom petals as being "cut out of hard moon shells, by a sharp knife of mother-of-pearl, into curving, scimitar petals."

Q23: What does Maya believe is the core of the night and their moods?

A) The clear night sky

B) Gautama's philosophical musings

C) The several scents, their varying essences and associations

D) The memories of Toto

Answer: C

Ex- Maya thinks that "the night filled with these several scents, their varying essences and associations, their effects on me, on us, were all important, the very core of the night, of our moods tonight."

Q24: What does Maya resign herself to after four years of marriage?

A) Gautama's lack of understanding

B) Her own increased capacity for philosophy

C) The inevitability of death

D) The loss of her ideals

Answer: B

Ex- After mentioning she and Gautama had been married for four years, Maya sighs "in resignation, for we had been married four years now... and I had developed a slightly greater capacity for philosophy than I was born with."

Q25: What interrupts Maya's thoughts about Toto's death?

A) The sound of a visitor arriving

B) Gautama's Urdu couplet

C) Gautama's speech about reality and idealism

D) The scent of the lemon blossoms

Answer: C

Ex- As Maya is thinking about Toto's death, the text states "Quickly he interrupted, his words cutting my thoughts away like a surgeon expertly removing a boil," leading into Gautama's speech about reality and idealism.

Q1: What does Gautama quote to Maya?

A) A Hindi proverb

B) An English poem

C) A Persian couplet

D) A Sanskrit verse

Answer: C

Ex- The text states that Gautama "quoted, softly, an Urdu couplet."

Q2: What is Maya's reaction to the couplet Gautama quotes?

A) She ignores it

B) She finds it meaningless

C) She is deeply moved by its compassion and understanding

D) She is confused by its meaning

Answer: C

Ex- Maya describes the couplet as "weighted with a rare compassion, a tender understanding," and it brings her a moment of "absolute happiness."

Q3: How does Maya describe her moment of happiness after hearing the couplet?

A) Fleeting and insignificant

B) Painful and agonizing, but also sweet and perfect

C) Calm and peaceful

D) Overwhelming and frightening

Answer: B

Ex- Maya's heart "stretched painfully, agonizingly," but she also describes it as "the ultimate, absolute joy" and "perfection."

Q4: Why does Maya insist on leaving the lights off in the dining room?

A) To save electricity

B) To create a romantic atmosphere

C) So they can see the stars while eating

D) Because she has a headache

Answer: C

Ex- Maya pleads, "They are so bright tonight," referring to the stars, and imagines that "from this dimness we should be able to look out on the stars."

Q5: What is Gautama's reaction to eating in the dark?

A) He finds it romantic
