B) He complains that it's too hot to eat

C) He enjoys the novelty of the experience

D) He suggests lighting candles instead

Answer: B

Ex- Gautama, "who was no romantic," grumbles that "it was too hot to eat" in the darkness.

Q6: What happens to dampen Maya's enthusiasm for the stars during dinner?

A) Gautama's complaint about the heat

B) A moth falling into Gautama's dessert

C) The sound of the servants' voices

D) The lack of a breeze through the windows

Answer: B

Ex- When "a clumsy, fluttering moth did fall into the iced sweet on Gautama's plate," Maya "rose and switched on the lights resignedly."

Q7: How does Maya describe her technique for getting into bed on summer nights?

A) Diving in quickly to avoid the heat

B) Slowly stretching out each limb and sighing deeply

C) Tossing and turning until she finds a comfortable position

D) Lying perfectly still to avoid sweating

Answer: B

Ex- Maya takes "a deep skin-diver's breath" and "very slowly stretched out one leg, then the other, first one arm, then the next, slowly let my breath out in a long sigh."

Q8: What does Gautama compare Maya to when she gets into bed?

A) A bird settling into its nest

B) A small, lazy cat

C) A fish swimming in water

D) A butterfly landing on a flower

Answer: B

Ex- During their early marriage, Gautama compared Maya to "A small lazy cat" when she got into bed.

Q9: What sound does Maya focus on as she lies in bed?

A) The ticking of a clock

B) The chirping of crickets

C) The chant of "Radha-Krishna" from the servants' quarters

D) The rustle of leaves in the breeze

Answer: C

Ex- Maya lets "the rhythm of the chant wash over me in swift, regular waves," referring to the servants' chant of "Radha-Krishna, Radha-Krishna."

Q10: How does Maya describe the night sky?

A) Intensely black and soft

B) Pale and uninspiring

C) Cloudy and overcast

D) Bright and painful to look at

Answer: A

Ex- Maya observes that "The sky was intensely black, intensely soft, so that one's eyes plunged and pierced."

Q11: What does Maya compare the Milky Way to?

A) A river of diamonds

B) A girdle binding the horizon

C) A path to heaven

D) A veil covering the earth

Answer: B

Ex- Maya describes the Milky Way as "that great, pale girdle that bound horizon to horizon."

Q12: What does Maya think about as she watches the stars?

A) The vastness of space and time

B) The beauty of nature

C) Her love for Gautama

D) The meaning of life

Answer: A

Ex- Maya thinks of "the immense distance between earth and stars, of the millions of years, the aeons that it took for their light to travel down the great vacuum into my waiting eyes."

Q13: What makes Maya feel restless as she lies in bed?

A) The heat of the summer night

B) The sound of the servants' chanting

C) Her thoughts about the stars and the vastness of space and time

D) The hardness of the mattress

Answer: C

Ex- Maya's "line of thought made me restless, not calm," referring to her reflections on the stars and the "loneliness of time, the impossible vastness of space."

Q14: What does Maya repeat to herself like a magic charm?

A) The servants' chant of "Radha-Krishna"

B) Gautama's Urdu couplet

C) The names of the constellations

D) A prayer for peace

Answer: B

Ex- Maya states, "Like a magic charm, I repeated Gautama's couplet to myself, the lovely words, trying to think of their richness and tenderness alone."

Q15: What does Maya wonder about her place in the universe?

A) If she has a place at all

B) If her place is insignificant

C) If her place is predetermined

D) If she definitely has a place

Answer: D

Ex- Maya reflects, "For there never was a doubt in my mind, now that I remember, as to the definite truth that I did indeed have a place in it."

Q16: Who does Maya think of along with herself when considering her place in the universe?

A) Gautama

B) The servants

C) Her small dog

D) Professor Rehman

Answer: C

Ex- Maya thinks, "Not only I, but my small dog, whose sad journey I followed with my mind," when considering her place in the universe.

Q17: What does Maya consider doing when she feels lonely and terrified?

A) Going for a walk outside

B) Waking up the servants

C) Going to Gautama's study to seek comfort

D) Writing in her diary

Answer: C

Ex- Maya wonders, "Dared I go in? Beg for comfort? Confess to my loneliness and my terror of loneliness?" referring to going to Gautama in his study.

Q18: What strange feeling has troubled Maya all day?

A) A sense of joy and peace

B) A feeling of boredom and restlessness

C) A sense of some forgotten or unknown disaster

D) A feeling of excitement and anticipation

Answer: C

Ex- Maya describes "A persistent sense of some disaster I had known, and forgotten, or perhaps never known, only, at one time, feared, and now rediscovered."

Q19: What does Maya compare this troubling feeling to?

A) A grain of sand itching on her skin

B) A thorn pricking her finger

C) A weight on her shoulders

D) A knot in her stomach

Answer: A

Ex- Maya describes the feeling as "A grain of sand itching, itching upon the tender infant skin of my new sorrow."

Q20: What does Maya sense beneath the sound of the drums and the scent of lemon blossoms?

A) A feeling of peace and contentment

B) A foreign odour and a struggle of despair

C) A sense of impending joy

D) A longing for adventure

Answer: B

Ex- Maya senses "a foreign odour amidst the scent of lemon blossoms, a struggle of despair that continued beyond the sleep of death."

Q21: How does Maya view the stars as she searches them for answers?

A) As a source of pure enchantment

B) As a puzzling message in an unknown language

C) As a comforting presence

D) As a meaningless decoration

Answer: B

Ex- Maya feels "as though I were faced with an important message in a language. I could not read," as she searches the stars.

Q22: What does Maya believe about the language of the stars?

A) It is impossible to understand

B) It is a language she has never encountered before

C) It is a language she once knew but has forgotten

D) It is a language only Gautama can interpret

Answer: C

Ex- Regarding the stars, Maya thinks, "It was a language I had once known, I needed only to remember."

Q23: What does Maya sense about the timing of deciphering the stars' message?

A) It is not important when she understands it

B) She has plenty of time to figure it out

C) She must understand it soon

D) It is already too late to understand

Answer: C

Ex- Maya believes she must remember the language of the stars soon, thinking, "But I must do so soon."

Q24: What questions does Maya ask herself about the stars' message?

A) "How? Why? And after that — what?"

B) "Who? What? When? Where? Why?"

C) "Is it real? Does it matter?"

D) "Will it change my life?"

Answer: A

Ex- The text directly states that Maya asks herself, "How? Why? And after that — what?" in relation to understanding the stars.

Q25: What is Maya's state of mind at the end of the passage?

A) Peaceful and content

B) Excited and hopeful

C) Confused and questioning

D) Angry and frustrated

Answer: C

Ex- The passage ends with Maya's unanswered questions about the language of the stars, suggesting she is still confused and searching for understanding.

### अभ्यास प्रश्न

1. What prevented Maya from fully releasing her tears?
   A. A sense of unease and hesitance
   B. Exhaustion from crying
   C. Lack of emotional connection to her pet
   D. Fear of expressing her emotions
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya mentions a "certain unease, a hesitance in the air" that kept her tears from being fully released.

2. What did Maya believe she was mourning, in addition to her pet's death?
   A. A forgotten memory
   B. An unremembered sorrow
   C. A future experience
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya admits she was mourning "another sorrow, unremembered, perhaps as yet not even experienced."

3. How did Gautama attempt to comfort Maya?
   A. By offering her an opal ring
   B. By suggesting she needs a cup of tea
   C. By telling her to go to sleep
   D. By holding her close and wiping her tears
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Maya was grieving, Gautama said, "You need a cup of tea," showing his lack of understanding of her misery.

4. What did Maya feel was the reason for her loneliness in the house?
   A. Gautama's hardness
   B. Gautama's coldness and emotional distance
   C. Gautama's incessant talk of philosophy
   D. Gautama's focus on his work
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya cried, "Yes, it is his hardness — no, no, not hardness, but the distance he coldly keeps from me. His coldness, his coldness..."

5. What did Maya do to exhaust herself after realizing her loneliness?
   A. Crying
   B. Pillow-beating and kicking
   C. Running around the house
   D. Screaming into a pillow
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya "gave myself up to a fit of furious pillow-beating, kicking, everything but crying" to exhaust herself.

6. What did Maya become more aware of when her wildest passions were over?
   A. The rhythm of her pulse
   B. The crepitations of the mind and the coldness of the heart
   C. The evening breeze coming through the window
   D. The distant thoughts streaking across her awareness
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya realized that when her greatest furies calmed, she "grew most keenly aware of the crepitations of the mind, the strange coldness of the heart."

7. What relationship is said to be no less worthy of reverence than that of a woman and her child?
   A. A woman and her husband
   B. A woman and her pet
   C. A woman and her best friend
   D. A woman and her sibling
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage states, "Childless women do develop fanatic attachments to their pets, they say. It is no less a relationship than that of a woman and her child, no less worthy of reverence, and agonized remembrance."

8. What did Maya promise to never forget?
   A. Her pet's name
   B. The fond names she had for her pet
   C. The moments she shared with her pet
   D. The love she had for her pet
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya promised, with parted lips and clasped hands, to never forget "the fond names I had had for my pet."

9. What did Maya turn to when abstract thinking was not successful for her?
   A. Remembrance of a lullaby from her childhood
   B. Thoughts of Gautama's tenderness
   C. Memories of her pet
   D. Imagining a future without her pet
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When abstract thinking was not successful, Maya "turned to remembrance of a lullaby that was crooned to me as a child a gentle, poignant lullaby that I murmured to yself."

10. How did Gautama's tenderness affect Maya when he came to her?
   A. She was filled with anger
   B. She was flooded with tenderness and gratitude
   C. She felt indifferent towards him
   D. She pushed him away
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Gautama touched her hair and spoke to her softly, Maya "was flooded with tenderness and gratitude."

11. What did Gautama suggest they do after Maya stopped crying?
   A. Stay in the bedroom
   B. Go for a walk in the garden
   C. Have dinner with the servants
   D. Discuss philosophy
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "Wipe your face, and we'll go out, Maya," suggesting they go outside in the garden.

12. How did the darkness in the garden affect Maya?
   A. It shocked her like a strong and effective medicine
   B. It made her feel agreeably like a child
   C. It reminded her of her pet's burial
   D. It made her feel more alone
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage states, "The balm of darkness met me with a little shock, like a strong and effective medicine on a wound still fresh."

13. What did Gautama do when Maya took his arm in the garden?
   A. He held her close
   B. He moved away, pretending to gesture to the gardener
   C. He told her he detested when she held his arm
   D. He led her back inside the house
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Maya took Gautama's arm, "He tolerated my hold for a moment, in consideration of my bereavement, I expect, then moved away, pretending to gesture to the gardener."

14. What were the rituals of the evening comforting to Maya?
   A. The preparations for the night
   B. The gardener sprinkling the lawn
   C. The servants taking the beds out
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage states, "The rituals of the evening, the preparations for the night, all were vastly comforting to me."

15. What prevented Maya from fully crying and finding relief in her tears?
   A. Gautama's presence
   B. The realization that she was mourning more than her pet's death
   C. The memory of her pet's soft, willing body
   D. The hardness of Gautama's pillow
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya admits that "it was not my pet's death alone that I mourned today, but another sorrow, unremembered, perhaps as yet not even experienced, and filled me with this despair."

16. What did Maya try to focus on to trace the source of her disturbance?
   A. The rhythm of her pulse
   B. The evening breeze coming through the window
   C. The distant and dark thoughts streaking across her awareness
   D. The fond names she had for her pet
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya "listened to the throb of my pulse" and tried to think of it as a rhythm that would carry her back to the source of her disturbance.

17. What did Maya realize about the concept of "never" when promising to never forget her pet?
   A. It was a meaningful promise
   B. It was linked to the chain of time
   C. It was an impossible promise to keep
   D. It was the only way to truly remember her pet
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya became "immediately made aware of the moments trickling past" and realized "There was no such thing as never. Only now. And yesterday."

18. What did Gautama do when he found Maya lying in the dark?
   A. He scolded her for not being outside
   B. He touched her hair and smoothed it down carefully
   C. He told her to stop crying and move on
   D. He ignored her and went about his evening routine
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Gautama found Maya lying in the dark, "he touched my hair, smoothing it down carefully as a nurse would."

19. What did Gautama take out to wipe Maya's face when she began crying again?
   A. A tissue
   B. A stained handkerchief
   C. A cloth napkin
   D. His sleeve
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Maya began crying again, Gautama "took out the handkerchief again, more stained than ever."

20. What did Gautama firmly help Maya do after wiping her face?
   A. Get out of bed
   B. Change her clothes
   C. Brush her hair
   D. Eat something
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: "Firmly he helped me out of bed, waited until I had found my slippers and smoothed my clothes, then led me out on the verandah, down the steps into the garden."

21. What did Maya know Gautama detested her doing?
   A. Crying in front of him
   B. Talking about her pet
   C. Taking his arm
   D. Going for walks in the garden
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Maya took Gautama's arm in the garden, she "knew he detested me to do so."

22. What was the gardener doing when Gautama and Maya went outside?
   A. Planting flowers
   B. Trimming the hedges
   C. Sprinkling the lawn
   D. Sweeping the verandah
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Gautama and Maya went outside, the gardener "was sprinkling the lawn so as to lay the dust before the beds were brought out."

23. What did Maya believe was the reason for Gautama's tolerance of her holding his arm?
   A. He genuinely wanted to comfort her
   B. He was considerate of her bereavement
   C. He enjoyed her touch
   D. He wanted to appear caring in front of the servants
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya believed Gautama "tolerated my hold for a moment, in consideration of my bereavement."

24. What did Gautama do after tolerating Maya's hold on his arm for a moment?
   A. He held her closer
   B. He gently removed her hand
   C. He moved away, pretending to gesture to the gardener
   D. He told her to let go
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: After tolerating Maya's hold for a moment, Gautama "moved away, pretending to gesture to the gardener."

25. What did Maya find vastly comforting at the end of the passage?
   A. Gautama's presence
   B. The darkness of the garden
   C. The rituals of the evening and preparations for the night
   D. The memory of her pet
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage states, "The rituals of the evening, the preparations for the night, all were vastly comforting to me."

26. Where were the verandah chairs taken out for Maya and Gautama to sit?
   A. On the porch
   B. On the lawn
   C. In the living room
   D. In the bedroom
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage states, "The verandah chairs had been taken out on the lawn for us."

27. What did Maya and Gautama do each evening?
   A. Have dinner together
   B. Watch the sunset
   C. Sit in comfortable cane chairs and have glasses of fresh lemonade
   D. Go for a walk in the garden
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya and Gautama sat down each evening "to glasses of fresh lemonade and to an hour or so of matrimonial silences and conversation."

28. What type of plant entwined the pillars and climbed the walls of the verandah?
   A. Jasmine
   B. Bougainvillea
   C. Rangoon creeper
   D. Honeysuckle
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: "Rangoon creeper entwined these pillars and climbed the walls, spread trembling tendrils towards the roof."

29. What color were the Rangoon creeper flowers in daylight?
   A. Pink and red
   B. White
   C. Yellow and orange
   D. Purple and blue
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The Rangoon creeper flowers "had been pink and red in daylight, and now were white and strongly scented."

30. What is said to attract snakes, according to the passage?
   A. The sweet, intoxicating fragrance of Rangoon creeper
   B. The sweet, intoxicating fragrance of Queen of the Night
   C. The sound of crickets chirping
   D. The presence of small rodents
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage states, "It is Queen of the Night that attracts snakes."

31. What season had just ended, and which season was yet to arrive?
   A. Autumn had ended, winter was yet to arrive
   B. Winter had ended, spring was yet to arrive
   C. Winter had ended, summer was yet to arrive
   D. Spring had ended, summer was yet to arrive
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: "Winter was over, summer had not yet arrived."

32. What did Maya feel was irking her at the back of her mind?
   A. A grain of sand
   B. A sense of unease
   C. A physical shadow
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya felt "that undefinable unease at the back of my mind, the grain of sand that irked, itched, and remained meaningless," and "its presence was very real, and a truly physical shadow."

33. What sensation overcame Maya, causing her to leap from her chair in terror?
   A. A sensation of snakes coiling and uncoiling their moist lengths about her
   B. A sensation of evil descending from an overhanging branch
   C. A sensation of an insane death, unprepared for, heralded by deafening drum-beats
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya was "overcome by a sensation of snakes coiling and uncoiling their moist lengths about me, of evil descending from an overhanging branch, of an insane death, unprepared for, heralded by deafening drum-beats."

34. What did Maya realize was causing the pounding sensation against her ear-drums?
   A. The sound of crickets
   B. The sound of her own blood
   C. The sound of drums in the distance
   D. The sound of the servants running towards her
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya realized the pounding sensation was "only been the blood pounding against my ear-drums."

35. What flew out of the tree when someone switched on a table-lamp?
   A. A bat
   B. A moth
   C. An owl
   D. A bird
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When someone switched on a table-lamp, "an owl flew out of the tree, hooting with merriment."

36. What did Maya insist on doing after the panic subsided?
   A. Sitting back down in the chair
   B. Going inside the house
   C. Walking up and down with Gautama
   D. Continuing the conversation with the servants
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya said, "But I wouldn't sit here. Let us walk, Gautama, let us walk up and down."

37. How did Gautama describe his voice when trying to reassure Maya?
   A. Reassuring, logical, and calm
   B. Soothing, gentle, and kind
   C. Firm, authoritative, and commanding
   D. Excited, animated, and lively
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama's voice was described as "reassuring, logical, calm."

38. What did Gautama suggest as a "straw to cling to" regarding the pet's death?
   A. The pet did not really suffer long
   B. The pet's death was peaceful
   C. The pet was old and had lived a full life
   D. The pet was now in a better place
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "If you would like a straw to cling to, then here is one — he did not suffer long."

39. What did Gautama believe the vet would have done if they had arrived?
   A. Saved the pet's life
   B. Eased the pet's suffering
   C. Prolonged the pet's pain
   D. Performed a miracle
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "The vet would only have prolonged the pain, in a case like this."

40. What did Maya know about the pet's condition, even though she wished the vet could have helped?
   A. The pet's condition was treatable
   B. The pet's condition was hopeless
   C. The pet's condition was improving
   D. The pet's condition was uncertain
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya "whimpered, knowing it had been hopeless" when she said, "He might have helped."

41. What sensation did Gautama's voice create in Maya's mind?
   A. A sensation of walking through a bright and sunny day
   B. A sensation of walking through a dark and wet night with somebody beside her who carried a lantern, a staff, and a blanket
   C. A sensation of floating on a calm and peaceful lake
   D. A sensation of being lost in a dense and confusing forest
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama's voice created "a sensation, a much loved, long courted sensation, of walking through a dark and wet night with somebody beside me who carried a lantern, a staff and a blanket" in Maya's mind.

42. What did Maya do instinctively when Gautama's voice created this sensation?
   A. Moved closer to him
   B. Moved away from him
   C. Stopped walking
   D. Started running
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Gautama's voice created the sensation, Maya "moved closer to him, instinctively."

43. What did Gautama stop to do while talking to Maya?
   A. Tie his shoelace
   B. Light a cigarette
   C. Adjust his shirt
   D. Check his watch
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama stopped "to light a cigarette" while talking to Maya.

44. What was Gautama sorry about regarding Maya's experience that day?
   A. That Maya had to witness the pet's death
   B. That Maya had been alone with the pet's body all day, with no one to put it away
   C. That Maya had to make the decision to euthanize the pet
   D. That Maya had to break the news of the pet's death to the family
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "I am only sorry about your having been alone with the body all day, with no one to put it away."

45. What did Gautama assume had happened to the pet's body by the time he and Maya were talking?
   A. It had been buried
   B. It had been cremated
   C. It had been taken away by the vet
   D. It was still in the house
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "But by now it must have been cremated."

46. What time of year was it when the events in the passage took place?
   A. January
   B. April
   C. July
   D. October
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage mentions "the dry, scented April dark" and "April stars," indicating the events took place in April.

47. What were the servants doing while Maya and Gautama were walking and talking?
   A. Sprinkling the lawn and smoothing sheets
   B. Preparing breakfast
   C. Cleaning the house
   D. Tending to the garden
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage states, "the servants went back to sprinkling the lawn, to smoothing sheets, to preparing dinner."

48. What did the Rangoon creeper flowers resemble when hanging in long bunches?
   A. Red grapes
   B. White grapes
   C. Cherries
   D. Plums
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The Rangoon creeper flowers "hung in long bunches, like those of white grapes."

49. What movement did the Rangoon creeper flowers make in the breeze?
   A. A slow, mysterious movement as of nocturnal snakes
   B. A fast, erratic movement as of startled birds
   C. A gentle, swaying movement as of ocean waves
   D. A circular, spinning movement as of falling leaves
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The Rangoon creeper flowers made "a slow, mysterious movement as of nocturnal snakes" when rising and descending upon the uneven breeze.

50. What did Maya compare the giant shadows cast by trees to, in terms of their effect on her?
   A. A soothing blanket of darkness
   B. A playful game of hide-and-seek
   C. A horrifying swiftness that reached her toes and lapped her feet
   D. A comforting embrace of nature
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The giant shadows cast by trees "spilt across the leaves and grasses towards me, with horrifying swiftness, till, like the crowding blades of grass, it reached my toes, lapped my feet, tickling and worrying."

51. How did Maya react when Gautama told her about the pet's body being cremated?
   A. She drew closer to him
   B. She drew away from him with a jerk
   C. She remained indifferent
   D. She started crying uncontrollably
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya "drew away from him with a jerk" and said, "No, stop. Why do you tell me this?"

52. How did Gautama view the ceremonies and trimmings associated with burying the dead?
   A. As meaningful and important
   B. As the ultimate absurdity and meaningless
   C. As a necessary evil
   D. As a way to honor the deceased
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama considered the ceremonies and trimmings associated with burying the dead "the ultimate absurdity, appropriate only in that it brings a meaningless life to a similarly meaningless end."

53. According to Gautama, what do people who have no greater values apply themselves to with passion?
   A. Lesser values that are not values at all
   B. Greater values that have a standard and criterion
   C. A mix of greater and lesser values
   D. No values at all
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "Having no greater values, they apply themselves with passion to the lesser ones — which are not values at all, of course, for values must have a standard, a criterion."

54. How did Maya listen to Gautama's philosophical musings?
   A. With full attention and agreement
   B. With complete disinterest and boredom
   C. Half-attentively and half-pensively, with her thoughts straying
   D. With anger and frustration
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya listened to Gautama "half-attentively, half-pensively, my thoughts straying from the straight, cobbled path of his march into side-lanes and by-lanes of my own choosing and my own making."

55. What did Maya sometimes offer to Gautama during their conversations?
   A. A handful of rushes or an oddly shaped pebble
   B. A cup of tea or a snack
   C. A book or a newspaper article
   D. A joke or a funny story
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya would sometimes return to Gautama "to see if he were still plodding along as he had been when I deserted him, coming to offer him a handful of rushes I had collected, or an oddly shaped pebble."

56. According to Gautama, whose lives are of importance and matter?
   A. The majority of dim-wits and numbskulls
   B. The few who lead the dim-wits and numbskulls, capable of logic and analysis
   C. Everyone, regardless of their intelligence or status
   D. No one's life matters or is of importance
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama believed that "It is only the few who lead these animals, the ones who are capable of logic and analysis, who matter. Their lives are of importance."

57. What did Gautama find confounding about intelligent, reasonable men?
   A. Their dedication to the solidity of thought and facts
   B. Their discussion of the beauty of burial vs. cremation with the same avidity as the "sheep"
   C. Their lack of interest in philosophical matters
   D. Their inability to lead the dim-wits and numbskulls
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama found it confounding that "intelligent, reasonable men — to discuss, with the same avidity the sheep have, the beauty of burial as opposed to the beauty of cremation, argue about it as though it were a fine point in theology."

58. What did Maya believe about burial and cremation, in contrast to Gautama's views?
   A. They are facts that matter
   B. They are meaningless and trivial
   C. They are only important for the wealthy and powerful
   D. They should be abolished altogether
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Gautama dismissed burial and cremation as trivial, Maya argued, "Burial and cremation are facts, Gautama."

59. What did Maya say about the importance of pets in her life?
   A. Pets are meaningless and do not matter
   B. Pets are only important for children
   C. Pets might not mean anything to Gautama, but they mean the world to her
   D. Pets are a waste of time and resources
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya cried, "Oh, Gautama, pets might not mean anything to you, and yet they mean the world to me."

60. What did Maya believe about the desire for a splendid death and being remembered?
   A. It is a foolish and vain desire
   B. It is a desire held by those who do not value living
   C. It is a desire that should be discouraged
   D. It is a desire held by those who want to be remembered for ages by everybody
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya said, "Living mightn't mean much to some people, but when they die, they want it to happen splendidly, something that will be remembered, for ages, by everybody."

61. How did Gautama react when Maya mentioned the desire for immortality?
   A. He was annoyed and dismissive
   B. He was delighted and approving
   C. He was confused and puzzled
   D. He was indifferent and uninterested
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: When Maya mentioned the desire for something that will be remembered, Gautama cried, "Ah!" and his white face sprung out "as though to pounce-upon something he had recognized, and approved."

62. According to Gautama, what is the only thing that continues to live?
   A. Wealth and power
   B. Tradition
   C. Intelligence and logic
   D. Love and compassion
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "the only thing that continues to live is tradition."

63. What does Gautama believe about traditions that have endured?
   A. They must be worthy of endurance
   B. They are all outdated and should be discarded
   C. They are only important for the older generation
   D. They have no value or meaning
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "Having endured, they must be worthy of endurance, claim these simple souls, and thus they find something they can dedicate their small silly minds to with impunity."

64. What does Maya insist is the reason why traditions matter to people?
   A. Because they are ancient and antediluvian
   B. Because they are fashionable and impressive
   C. Because they matter to them personally
   D. Because they are easy to follow without thinking
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya insisted, "It is also because it matters to them."

65. What does Gautama believe the Gita preaches or recommends?
   A. Involvement in tradition
   B. Detachment on every count
   C. Worship of plaster gods
   D. Empty rites and rituals
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "The Gita does not preach involvement in tradition. It preaches — recommends, rather — detachment on every count."

66. What does Gautama believe would happen if people truly understood the Gita?
   A. They would become more involved in traditions
   B. They would worship plaster gods more fervently
   C. They would be capable of disappearing without the paraphernalia of vulgarity
   D. They would become more attached to empty rites and rituals
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Gautama said, "If only those who read it so piously, in front of their plaster gods, every morning and every night, would also understand it, then they would be capable of disappearing without drawing down upon their heads this paraphernalia of vulgarity."

67. How did Maya feel about Gautama's use of the word "disappearance"?
   A. She found it comforting and reassuring
   B. She found it cruel and painful
   C. She found it amusing and funny
   D. She found it inspiring and motivating
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya felt "a pang as the solidity of real existence gave forth one little gasp and collapsed, like a worm-eaten fruit, at my feet" when Gautama spoke of disappearance, calling it "a cruel word, cruelly spoken."

68. What memories of Toto did Maya think of in response to Gautama's words?
   A. Toto's short stump of a white tail and the foolish little wisp at its end
   B. Toto's laziness and lack of energy
   C. Toto's aggression and bad behavior
   D. Toto's indifference to Maya's presence
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: In response to Gautama's words, Maya "thought of Toto's short stump of a white tail again, of the foolish little wisp at its end."

69. How did Toto react when Maya returned from a morning in town?
   A. He ignored her and continued sleeping
   B. He barked wildly and thrilledly, flinging himself upon her
   C. He growled and showed aggression towards her
   D. He hid from her and avoided contact
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya remembered "Toto's wild, thrilled bark as he saw me return from a morning in town, the impact of his body as he flung himself upon me."

70. What did Maya believe about the endurance of her memories of Toto compared to Gautama's "facts"?
   A. Her memories were less enduring than Gautama's facts
   B. Her memories were at least as enduring as Gautama's facts
   C. Her memories had no endurance at all
   D. Her memories were completely unrelated to Gautama's facts
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya believed that her memories of Toto "did not disappear" and "were at least as enduring as the 'facts' Gautama was continually extolling."

71. Why did Maya not ask Gautama aloud about the endurance of her memories?
   A. She was afraid of his reaction
   B. She thought her private thoughts were sufficient
   C. She did not care about his opinion
   D. She agreed with his perspective
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya did not ask Gautama aloud about the endurance of her memories because "These were private thoughts and having thought them was sufficient."

72. What did Maya believe she had to learn to be grateful for in her relationship with Gautama?
   A. His intelligence and logical thinking
   B. His presence alone, not the meanderings of his brain
   C. His involvement in traditions and rituals
   D. His emotional support and understanding
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya realized that "she had to learn to be grateful for his presence alone, not be disturbed by the meanderings of his brain."

73. How did Gautama's brain work when he was with Maya, according to the passage?
   A. Like a clock spring winding itself tightly
   B. Like a clock spring unwinding itself into relaxation
   C. Like a well-oiled machine, efficiently and effectively
   D. Like a rusty gear, struggling to function properly
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: The passage states that Gautama's brain, "when with me, worked like a clock spring unwinding itself into relaxation."

74. What did Maya compare the collapse of the solidity of real existence to, based on Gautama's words?
   A. A worm-eaten fruit
   B. A deflated balloon
   C. A shattered glass
   D. A melted ice cube
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Maya felt that "the solidity of real existence gave forth one little gasp and collapsed, like a worm-eaten fruit, at my feet" in response to Gautama's words.

75. How did Maya respond to Gautama's philosophical musings and arguments?
   A. She agreed with him completely and followed his logic
   B. She disagreed with him vehemently and argued against his points
   C. She listened half-attentively, with her thoughts straying, and occasionally interrupted him
   D. She ignored him completely and changed the subject
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Explanation: Throughout the passage, Maya listens to Gautama "half-attentively, half-pensively," with her thoughts straying, and occasionally interrupts him with her own perspectives and arguments.

76. What flowers does Maya notice in the garden?
   A. Roses
   B. Petunias
   C. Daffodils
   D. Tulips
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya specifically mentions noticing petunias, describing them as "sentimental, irresolute flowers."

77. How does Maya describe the scent of the petunias?
   A. Crisp and refreshing
   B. Poignant, half-sweet, and half-sad
   C. Strong and vivid
   D. Sour and stringent
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya describes the petunias' fragrance as "a poignant, half-sweet, half-sad fragrance that matched my mood to perfection."

78. What does Gautama compare the scent of the lemon blossoms to?
   A. Ground lemon peel
   B. Crushed lemon leaf
   C. Mother-of-pearl
   D. Moon shells
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- When Maya tries to explain the scent of the lemon blossoms, she describes it as "refreshing as that of ground lemon peel."

79. How long have Maya and Gautama been married?
   A. One year
   B. Two years
   C. Three years
   D. Four years
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya and Gautama "had been married four years now."

80. What does Maya believe Gautama's visitor wanted to discuss?
   A. A case of passion and revenge
   B. A murder case
   C. Money or property
   D. Exciting things
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya assumes Gautama's visitor wanted to discuss "some musty old case, about money, or property, or something dreary like that."

81. According to Gautama, what does love ultimately become for a man?
   A. Worrying about bills
   B. Dealing with bills
   C. Rearing children
   D. Paying for children's upbringing
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gautama states that love "ultimately becomes, you will find, for the man a matter or dealing with the bills that come in."

82. What does Gautama say about the relationship between reality and idealism?
   A. They are distinct and separate
   B. They are one and the same thing
   C. Reality is more important than idealism
   D. Idealism is more important than reality
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gautama explains that "reality and idealism are one and the same thing" and that life is about reconciling them.

83. What does Maya compare her thoughts of anguish to?
   A. Flowers in a garden
   B. Stars in the night sky
   C. Birds waking from dreams
   D. Petals on a flower
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya's thoughts of anguish are described as rising "like birds that awake from dreams and rise out of their trees amidst great commotion."

84. What does Maya focus on to draw away from her pain?
   A. The night sky
   B. The stone urn
   C. The white petunias
   D. Gautama's presence
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya bends over the white petunias, "inhaling that mist of sad, maidenly scent," to draw away from her pain.

85. What does Gautama say about the night sky?
   A. It's a clear night
   B. The stars are beautiful
   C. The darkness is overwhelming
   D. It reminds him of loneliness
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gautama looks up at the night sky and simply remarks, "What a clear night."

86. What does Maya associate with the spaces of darkness between the stars?
   A. Beauty
   B. Hope
   C. Death
   D. Rebirth
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya thinks that "Death lurked in those spaces, the darkness spoke of distance, separation, loneliness."

87. What does Maya imagine the journey of the dead to be like?
   A. A short trip to a new life
   B. A brave traversing of mute darkness
   C. A joyful experience
   D. A journey filled with light
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya thinks of "the long journey of the dead from one birth into another, the brave traversing of mute darkness."

88. Who is Toto?
   A. Maya's husband
   B. Maya's friend
   C. Maya's pet
   D. Gautama's visitor
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Toto is mentioned as a small white corpse, implying he was Maya's pet who died.

89. How does Maya describe Toto's barks before his death?
   A. Loud and energetic
   B. Soft and comforting
   C. Futile and dying away into silence
   D. Happy and playful
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya remembers "Small white Toto, whining a little, out of fear, his futile barks dying away into a hopeless silence."

90. What does Maya confess to Gautama about Toto?
   A. She never liked Toto
   B. She will miss Toto terribly
   C. She blamed Gautama for Toto's death
   D. She doesn't want another pet
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya cries out to Gautama, "I shall miss him so terribly," referring to Toto.

91. How does Gautama respond to Maya's confession about missing Toto?
   A. He ignores her
   B. He scolds her for being emotional
   C. He wipes away her tear
   D. He walks away
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gautama raises his hand to wipe away Maya's tear as she confesses she will miss Toto terribly.

92. What is Gautama's hand compared to as he wipes away Maya's tear?
   A. A soft petal
   B. A cool breeze
   C. The bark of an old and shady tree
   D. A gentle wave
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gautama's hand is described as being "as cool and dry as the bark of an old and shady tree" as he wipes Maya's tear.

93. In what language does Gautama quote a couplet?
   A. Hindi
   B. English
   C. Urdu
   D. Sanskrit
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- After wiping Maya's tear, Gautama "quoted, softly, an Urdu couplet."

94. What does Maya find shocking about Gautama's speech?
   A. His lack of emotion
   B. His poetic language
   C. The reduction of ideals to mundane superfluities
   D. His agreement with her viewpoint
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya finds it "rather shocking to reduce one's ideals to mundane superfluities," as Gautama does in his speech.

95. What does Maya believe death reveals ideals to be?
   A. Meaningless
   B. The same as the superfluities one has known and spurned
   C. More important than reality
   D. Separate from reality
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya realizes that it is "equally shocking to realize that these superfluities are the ideals themselves."

96. What season has recently ended in the story?
   A. Spring
   B. Summer
   C. Autumn
   D. Winter
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions "a poignant, half-sweet, half-sad fragrance that matched my mood to perfection, and I started to it with the embrace of recognition, taking in deep breaths of this gentle, fading odour that was so laden with wistful remembrance of the winter."

97. How does Maya describe the shape of the lemon blossom petals?
   A. Round and soft
   B. Triangular and pointed
   C. Curving and scimitar-like
   D. Flat and oval
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya describes the lemon blossom petals as being "cut out of hard moon shells, by a sharp knife of mother-of-pearl, into curving, scimitar petals."

98. What does Maya believe is the core of the night and their moods?
   A. The clear night sky
   B. Gautama's philosophical musings
   C. The several scents, their varying essences and associations
   D. The memories of Toto
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya thinks that "the night filled with these several scents, their varying essences and associations, their effects on me, on us, were all important, the very core of the night, of our moods tonight."

99. What does Maya resign herself to after four years of marriage?
   A. Gautama's lack of understanding
   B. Her own increased capacity for philosophy
   C. The inevitability of death
   D. The loss of her ideals
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- After mentioning she and Gautama had been married for four years, Maya sighs "in resignation, for we had been married four years now... and I had developed a slightly greater capacity for philosophy than I was born with."

100. What interrupts Maya's thoughts about Toto's death?
   A. The sound of a visitor arriving
   B. Gautama's Urdu couplet
   C. Gautama's speech about reality and idealism
   D. The scent of the lemon blossoms
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- As Maya is thinking about Toto's death, the text states "Quickly he interrupted, his words cutting my thoughts away like a surgeon expertly removing a boil," leading into Gautama's speech about reality and idealism.

101. What does Gautama quote to Maya?
   A. A Hindi proverb
   B. An English poem
   C. A Persian couplet
   D. A Sanskrit verse
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Gautama "quoted, softly, an Urdu couplet."

102. What is Maya's reaction to the couplet Gautama quotes?
   A. She ignores it
   B. She finds it meaningless
   C. She is deeply moved by its compassion and understanding
   D. She is confused by its meaning
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya describes the couplet as "weighted with a rare compassion, a tender understanding," and it brings her a moment of "absolute happiness."

103. How does Maya describe her moment of happiness after hearing the couplet?
   A. Fleeting and insignificant
   B. Painful and agonizing, but also sweet and perfect
   C. Calm and peaceful
   D. Overwhelming and frightening
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya's heart "stretched painfully, agonizingly," but she also describes it as "the ultimate, absolute joy" and "perfection."

104. Why does Maya insist on leaving the lights off in the dining room?
   A. To save electricity
   B. To create a romantic atmosphere
   C. So they can see the stars while eating
   D. Because she has a headache
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya pleads, "They are so bright tonight," referring to the stars, and imagines that "from this dimness we should be able to look out on the stars."

105. What is Gautama's reaction to eating in the dark?
   A. He finds it romantic
   B. He complains that it's too hot to eat
   C. He enjoys the novelty of the experience
   D. He suggests lighting candles instead
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gautama, "who was no romantic," grumbles that "it was too hot to eat" in the darkness.

106. What happens to dampen Maya's enthusiasm for the stars during dinner?
   A. Gautama's complaint about the heat
   B. A moth falling into Gautama's dessert
   C. The sound of the servants' voices
   D. The lack of a breeze through the windows
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- When "a clumsy, fluttering moth did fall into the iced sweet on Gautama's plate," Maya "rose and switched on the lights resignedly."

107. How does Maya describe her technique for getting into bed on summer nights?
   A. Diving in quickly to avoid the heat
   B. Slowly stretching out each limb and sighing deeply
   C. Tossing and turning until she finds a comfortable position
   D. Lying perfectly still to avoid sweating
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya takes "a deep skin-diver's breath" and "very slowly stretched out one leg, then the other, first one arm, then the next, slowly let my breath out in a long sigh."

108. What does Gautama compare Maya to when she gets into bed?
   A. A bird settling into its nest
   B. A small, lazy cat
   C. A fish swimming in water
   D. A butterfly landing on a flower
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- During their early marriage, Gautama compared Maya to "A small lazy cat" when she got into bed.

109. What sound does Maya focus on as she lies in bed?
   A. The ticking of a clock
   B. The chirping of crickets
   C. The chant of "Radha-Krishna" from the servants' quarters
   D. The rustle of leaves in the breeze
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya lets "the rhythm of the chant wash over me in swift, regular waves," referring to the servants' chant of "Radha-Krishna, Radha-Krishna."

110. How does Maya describe the night sky?
   A. Intensely black and soft
   B. Pale and uninspiring
   C. Cloudy and overcast
   D. Bright and painful to look at
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya observes that "The sky was intensely black, intensely soft, so that one's eyes plunged and pierced."

111. What does Maya compare the Milky Way to?
   A. A river of diamonds
   B. A girdle binding the horizon
   C. A path to heaven
   D. A veil covering the earth
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya describes the Milky Way as "that great, pale girdle that bound horizon to horizon."

112. What does Maya think about as she watches the stars?
   A. The vastness of space and time
   B. The beauty of nature
   C. Her love for Gautama
   D. The meaning of life
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya thinks of "the immense distance between earth and stars, of the millions of years, the aeons that it took for their light to travel down the great vacuum into my waiting eyes."

113. What makes Maya feel restless as she lies in bed?
   A. The heat of the summer night
   B. The sound of the servants' chanting
   C. Her thoughts about the stars and the vastness of space and time
   D. The hardness of the mattress
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya's "line of thought made me restless, not calm," referring to her reflections on the stars and the "loneliness of time, the impossible vastness of space."

114. What does Maya repeat to herself like a magic charm?
   A. The servants' chant of "Radha-Krishna"
   B. Gautama's Urdu couplet
   C. The names of the constellations
   D. A prayer for peace
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya states, "Like a magic charm, I repeated Gautama's couplet to myself, the lovely words, trying to think of their richness and tenderness alone."

115. What does Maya wonder about her place in the universe?
   A. If she has a place at all
   B. If her place is insignificant
   C. If her place is predetermined
   D. If she definitely has a place
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya reflects, "For there never was a doubt in my mind, now that I remember, as to the definite truth that I did indeed have a place in it."

116. Who does Maya think of along with herself when considering her place in the universe?
   A. Gautama
   B. The servants
   C. Her small dog
   D. Professor Rehman
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya thinks, "Not only I, but my small dog, whose sad journey I followed with my mind," when considering her place in the universe.

117. What does Maya consider doing when she feels lonely and terrified?
   A. Going for a walk outside
   B. Waking up the servants
   C. Going to Gautama's study to seek comfort
   D. Writing in her diary
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya wonders, "Dared I go in? Beg for comfort? Confess to my loneliness and my terror of loneliness?" referring to going to Gautama in his study.

118. What strange feeling has troubled Maya all day?
   A. A sense of joy and peace
   B. A feeling of boredom and restlessness
   C. A sense of some forgotten or unknown disaster
   D. A feeling of excitement and anticipation
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya describes "A persistent sense of some disaster I had known, and forgotten, or perhaps never known, only, at one time, feared, and now rediscovered."

119. What does Maya compare this troubling feeling to?
   A. A grain of sand itching on her skin
   B. A thorn pricking her finger
   C. A weight on her shoulders
   D. A knot in her stomach
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya describes the feeling as "A grain of sand itching, itching upon the tender infant skin of my new sorrow."

120. What does Maya sense beneath the sound of the drums and the scent of lemon blossoms?
   A. A feeling of peace and contentment
   B. A foreign odour and a struggle of despair
   C. A sense of impending joy
   D. A longing for adventure
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya senses "a foreign odour amidst the scent of lemon blossoms, a struggle of despair that continued beyond the sleep of death."

121. How does Maya view the stars as she searches them for answers?
   A. As a source of pure enchantment
   B. As a puzzling message in an unknown language
   C. As a comforting presence
   D. As a meaningless decoration
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya feels "as though I were faced with an important message in a language. I could not read," as she searches the stars.

122. What does Maya believe about the language of the stars?
   A. It is impossible to understand
   B. It is a language she has never encountered before
   C. It is a language she once knew but has forgotten
   D. It is a language only Gautama can interpret
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Regarding the stars, Maya thinks, "It was a language I had once known, I needed only to remember."

123. What does Maya sense about the timing of deciphering the stars' message?
   A. It is not important when she understands it
   B. She has plenty of time to figure it out
   C. She must understand it soon
   D. It is already too late to understand
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya believes she must remember the language of the stars soon, thinking, "But I must do so soon."

124. What questions does Maya ask herself about the stars' message?
   A. "How? Why? And after that — what?"
   B. "Who? What? When? Where? Why?"
   C. "Is it real? Does it matter?"
   D. "Will it change my life?"
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text directly states that Maya asks herself, "How? Why? And after that — what?" in relation to understanding the stars.

125. What is Maya's state of mind at the end of the passage?
   A. Peaceful and content
   B. Excited and hopeful
   C. Confused and questioning
   D. Angry and frustrated
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The passage ends with Maya's unanswered questions about the language of the stars, suggesting she is still confused and searching for understanding. Chapter 2

## Main study material

Chapter 2

Q1: What color was the cat that Gautama gave to the narrator?

A) Black

B) Grey

C) White

D) Brown

Answer: C

Ex- The passage states: "Gautama gave me a cat. She was white and had hair like tassels of silk."

Q2: What stone was the color of the cat's eyes compared to?

A) Emerald

B) Topaz

C) Sapphire

D) Ruby

Answer: B

Ex- The cat's eyes are described as "topaz, with undertones of grey and overtones of green."

Q3: Where did the cat like to sit for long hours?

A) On the bed

B) In the garden

C) On the verandah steps

D) By the window

Answer: C

Ex- The passage mentions: "She would sit long hours on the verandah steps, gazing at the moon."

Q4: What bird's call was heard each morning at dawn?

A) Cuckoo

B) Brainfever bird

C) Parrot

D) Peacock

Answer: B

Ex- "As ever, Spring came with the brainfever bird, whose long, insistent call rang imploringly from the tree tops all morning and, again, at twilight."

Q5: What fruits did they have for breakfast?

A) Oranges and bananas

B) Apples and pears

C) Papayas sprinkled with lemon and sugar

D) Mangoes and pineapples

Answer: C

Ex- They breakfasted "on tea and papayas sprinkled with lemon and sugar."

Q6: What trees were the first to flower?

A) Neem trees

B) Mango trees

C) Silk-cotton trees

D) Banyan trees

Answer: C

Ex- "Down the street the silk-cotton trees were the first to flower."

Q7: What color were the silk-cotton tree flowers?

A) Yellow

B) Pink

C) Scarlet

D) Orange

Answer: C

Ex- The silk-cotton trees had "huge, scarlet blooms, thick-petalled, solid podded."

Q8: What filled the silk-cotton tree pods?

A) Seeds

B) Nectar

C) Silk cotton

D) Pollen

Answer: C

Ex- "Their pods were crowded with silk cotton."

Q9: Who gathered the silk cotton into sacks?

A) Children

B) Gardeners

C) Old women

D) Farmers

Answer: C

Ex- "Old women gathered them into sacks to sell to manufacturers of pillows and babies' quilts."

Q10: What type of bird nests were on the verandah?

A) Sparrow nests

B) Crow nests

C) Pigeon nests

D) Swallow nests

Answer: C

Ex- "On our verandah, the pigeon's nests were suddenly filled with babies."

Q11: What creepers rattled against the wall in gusts of wind?

A) Jasmine

B) Bougainvillaea

C) Honeysuckle

D) Ivy

Answer: B

Ex- "Gusts of wind dragged the thorny, paper-flowered bougainvillaea creepers against the wall."

Q12: Where did Maya want to go to see the Kathakali dances?

A) North India

B) East India

C) South India

D) West India

Answer: C

Ex- Maya says, "I want-I want to go south— to Travancore, Cochin, Mysore, Malabar, all those places," to see the Kathakali dances.

Q13: What did Maya's father advise her regarding facing difficulties in life?

A) To fight against them

B) To ignore them

C) To accept them

D) To find a solution

Answer: C

Ex- Maya's father told her, "If one can do nothing about them, why not accept?" when it came to life's difficulties.

Q14: What reminded Maya of opium?

A) Leila's eyes

B) Leila's hair

C) Leila's wrist

D) Leila's lips

Answer: C

Ex- Maya touched Leila's "white wrist slashed with azure veins" and said, "Perhaps you make me think of opium, Leila."

Q15: What was Leila's husband dying of?

A) Cancer

B) Heart disease

C) Tuberculosis

D) Pneumonia

Answer: C

Ex- Leila's husband "lay, dying of tuberculosis, upon his bed in one corner of their single room."

Q16: What subject did Leila teach?

A) English literature

B) Sanskrit literature

C) Persian literature

D) Hindi literature

Answer: C

Ex- Leila taught "Persian literature to a handful of girls" at the college.

Q17: What color saris did Pom's husband Kailash always buy for her?

A) Red

B) Blue

C) Yellow

D) Green

Answer: D

Ex- Pom complained, "Whenever Kailash buys me a new sari, it is always green."

Q18: What did Pom collect in her room?

A) Painted clay birds and fruits

B) Glass figurines

C) Porcelain dolls

D) Stuffed animals

Answer: A

Ex- Pom's bright room had "rows of painted clay birds and fruits that she collected."

Q19: What drink did Pom's husband have from a silver tumbler?

A) Tea

B) Coffee

C) Lassi

D) Juice

Answer: C

Ex- "Pom's husband would drink lassi from a silver tumbler."

Q20: What type of seeds were buried in the fresh bread Pom's mother-in-law made?

A) Sesame seeds

B) Poppy seeds

C) Pomegranate seeds

D) Pumpkin seeds

Answer: C

Ex- Pom's mother-in-law "would bring in fresh bread in which pomegranate seeds lay buried."

Q21: What pickles did Pom search for in the jars?

A) Mango, garlic, carrot

B) Tamarind, chilli, lime and venison

C) Onion, tomato, cucumber

D) Cauliflower, beans, beetroot

Answer: B

Ex- "Pom would search through jars of pickles - tamarind, chilli, lime and venison."

Q22: When was Pom expecting her baby?

A) September

B) October

C) November

D) December

Answer: C

Ex- Pom told Maya, "I'm to have a baby - in November."

Q23: On which day of the week did Pom take flowers to the temple?

A) Monday

B) Tuesday

C) Wednesday

D) Thursday

Answer: D

Ex- Pom said, "I take flowers to the temple every Thursday."

Q24: What gender baby did Pom want?

A) Girl

B) Boy

C) Twins

D) She didn't mention

Answer: B

Ex- Regarding her pregnancy, Pom said "I want it to be a boy."

Q25: Which temple did Pom visit to pray for a son?

A) Akshardham Temple

B) Lotus Temple

C) Birla Mandir

D) Iskcon Temple

Answer: C

Ex- When Maya asked where they were going, Pom replied, "Birla Mandir."
